And regarding the circular reasoning that one can only test a hypothesis within the given framework of the hypothesis: Just consider the hypothesis that solar energy is based on nuclear fusion and that this manifests in solar neutrinos. To verify this, as already explained in Section 2.a., one must build gigantic detectors in deep mines; an enormous effort that costs vast sums. This is only done if one is convinced of the validity of the hypothesis. If one is not, for example regarding orgone, one tests the orgone hypothesis "scientifically" in ORANUR-contaminated laboratories with non-functioning orgone energy accumulators on foggy and rainy days.

www.orgonomie.net                                         52

3.c. The Hard-to-Grasp Orgone

Electrical and electronic equipment can lead to an over-excitation of orgone energy (ORANUR) in the respective space, and accordingly the inhabitants become irritated, they behave in an agitated manner—until their organism gives up and nothing matters to them anymore. This explains a good part of the behavior of children in school and their parents in the office and during the drive in the electric car. A "mechanical atmosphere" is created, and not just in a figurative sense, but literally. But how to make someone in this mechano-mystical culture understand this?

For example, when I go into the forest, I sense the energetic difference between young plantations and old stands. From clusters of young trees that are fighting for their survival, i.e., for light, and therefore growing as fast as possible, one senses an anxious-nervous atmosphere emanating. Whereas in forest sections with large trees one perceives a majestic calm. The orgonotic emanation is completely, even drastically different.

It is difficult to describe. Like explaining the taste of a tomato to someone who has never eaten a tomato? How to explain "energetic" differences to people from whom such perceptions have been driven out since childhood and who were then "scientifically" drilled in schools? How to convey it to people who live in school, in the office, in means of transport, today even at home in a literally "mechanical atmosphere" and whose own body orgone has accordingly become mechanical itself?

Added to this is that through entertainment media and through computers they are increasingly conditioned to only see and hear. The energetic sensitivity, which is exceptionally developed in blind people for instance, is visibly withering. The human being becomes a robot with "sensors." A robot that no longer needs a living atmosphere.

That is the real misery of the technologized world: that what is decisive about the problem cannot be conveyed; rather, one makes a fool of oneself in the attempt to convey it, someone who cannot be taken seriously.


---


I am writing this after using a small orgone blanket, which had been stored dry in an airy forest cabin over the winter, on the first warm sunny day. I immediately sense that it is energetically active. But how to describe this "sensing"? When I had laid it on my chest, a pleasant, cozy feeling came over me. Also striking was a very agreeable smell of the blanket, such as is characteristic of fresh orgone energy. How does one convey such a thing to a "skeptic"? How to make comprehensible the feeling one has when one has had enough of the "radiation"?

All orgonomic experiments aim at coaxing out the "living" phenomena being sought, whereas mechanistic science is a "death-dealing enterprise." One need only think of the problem of pleomorphism — which falls under the table because all microbiological samples are disposed of (!) as "contaminated" after two days or so. One must give the living the possibility

www.orgonomie.net
                                         53

of unfolding itself — while Popperian scientism does everything in its power to kill life. A science that is "sterile" in the truest sense of the word.

Take sexuality and sleep: science can explain both phenomena very neatly and completely without orgone. There is only one small problem. If I want to know whether and what plants need water for, I simply stop watering them and immediately have the answer to both my questions. With sexuality and sleep it is different. There are people who get along splendidly without any sexuality whatsoever, and sleep-deprivation experiments cannot clearly establish precisely what function sleep has. If one disregards reproduction, both phenomena seem to be as superfluous as orgone itself! They clearly appear only in higher animals. And this is precisely where the crux lies. The functions of energy discharge (the function of the orgasm) and energy recharge (sleep) acquire an all-determining significance only in the highly energetic human being.

Astronomy and meteorology: in the textbooks one finds hardly any gaps that would cry out for orgone in order to explain cosmic and atmospheric phenomena. Here again it is an "extra" in which orgone shows itself. For example, according to Newton, planets and moons could move in all sorts of possible orbits, but for inexplicable reasons they preferentially circle the central bodies in accordance with Bode's law. The inclination of the planets toward the ecliptic points to the influence of the Galactic Orgone Energy Stream (Harman 1993). There are the various orgonotic parameters that change with the weather, and last but not least there is the cloudbuster. As far as stellar and weather systems are concerned, Reich wrote an entire book about this, Cosmic Superimposition (Reich 1951a).


---


Physics and biology: Quantum electrodynamics completely explains our everyday environment, Newton and Einstein have definitively described gravitation, and the nuclear physicists have radioactivity fully under control. Life can be explained chemically through the genes, and therefore mechanically. Where should there be room for orgone in all this? Orgone shows itself consistently in the "extra." – I am writing this in the evening, deep in the forest. As I look up, I see between the trees the orgone shimmering, excited by the sunset, in which I sit like a fish in water. At the moment humanity is doing everything it can, by means of ORANUR, to prove quite practically that the forest can also exist without atmospheric orgone energy…

3.d. Experiment and Theory Formation
In its January 1988 issue, the Zeitschrift für Sexualmedizin (Journal of Sexual Medicine) stated that Digne Meller Marcovicz's documentary film Wilhelm Reich: Viva Kleiner Mann (1987) was "an impressive documentary video, excellently assembled (…) The Museum of Modern Art, N.Y., wants to include the film (…) in its program." I found the film, like most modern "art," merely stupid and "forced." It was a film more as an "artistic" expression by Frau Meller Marcovicz than a film about Reich.

The film's title, the brutal way in which Meller Marcovicz handled the material, and certain echoes of Dusan Makavejev's botched work WR – Mysteries of the Organism reveal a deeply seated contempt for Reich. I also noticed a certain anti-American undertone – and the ending of the film in the USSR!

It was good to see Reich's coworkers, even though one heard hardly anything from, for example, Jo Jenks, which is not only a filmic disgrace! Otherwise I found all the people numbing: Peter Mills, the strange "vegetotherapists," Reimund Reiche, and, as the crowning touch of all repulsiveness: Volkmar Sigusch.

In Sexualmedizin, Sigusch is quoted as follows:

An experimental physicist from a Max Planck Institute repeated
                     these orgone experiments in the laboratory together with me.
                     After these precise measurements, the result was such that
                     nothing remained of Wilhelm Reich's claim (…).

To this, the journal's comment: "So, no orgone."

I could present any number of physics professors who show that, based on Reich's experiments, no new form of energy is to be assumed in the first place. There is much to say about this. First of all, statements that the respective field of science is complete recur regularly throughout the history of science. For example, the physicists at the end of the 19th century—that is, immediately before Planck and Einstein—were convinced that physics was, in principle, largely complete (like Newtonian optics, for instance), and that, with all minor refinements and the closing of a few gaps, there would never again be any fundamental restructuring of the theoretical edifice until the end of all time.


---


The training of a physicist begins with the "catechism" of classical mechanics, electromagnetism, thermodynamics, quantum physics (each supplemented relativistically) — at every university in the same sequence and with almost identical words. A hermetically self-contained worldview. That this is not physics one only realizes when one has to solve supposedly "simple" problems with true-to-scale models and elaborate computer programs, for example the design of a hydrodynamically optimal shape for a concrete ship's hull. In the textbooks celestial mechanics seems simple, but when one engages with it concretely,

www.orgonomie.net
                                         55

one asks oneself how on earth the planetary system has been able to persist for billions of years. Real physics one must work out laboriously. It is, so to speak, your personal relationship to matter.

A similar attitude, detached from the object of research, was until recently adopted by biology. Yet it is becoming increasingly apparent that the all-trampling triumphal procession of genetics, for all its astonishing and lasting insights, is running aground in epigenetics, which finally looks again at the protoplasm, i.e., at that which is actually living. And as for physics: James DeMeo has measured between 100 and 500 cpm, up to as high as 4000 cpm, with an orgone-charged neutron counter (DeMeo 2006). Normal is less than 5 cpm! These cannot be "neutrons" in the usual sense. Rather, conversely, the question of what neutrons and other subatomic particles actually are arises with renewed urgency!

Science is in constant flux: yesterday's certainties are today hardly more than curiosities. In Ether, God and Devil, Reich speaks of the role of "the Absolute," which is a correspondence of the immovable character structure of the armored human being (Reich 1949a).

It is quite simple and cheap to criticize Reich. He writes on this: "The exploration of new territories positively requires — let us say it calmly — a certain recklessness and a certain unconcern in one's approach" (Reich 1938, p. 173). I have absolutely no understanding when freshly graduated would-be scientists dig up every small error in Reich and thereby want to run down Reich's great discoveries. Even during his lifetime he was annoyed by the objections and control experiments of the know-it-alls. See, for example, the relevant passages in Beyond Psychology (Reich 1997). Reich writes on this:


---


Solely and alone the independent development and logic
                     of my experiments kept me at it. It is
                     interesting and useful to look back on such
                     uncertainties when the strangest phenomena
                     have become one's accustomed daily work. This
                     gives the necessary courage not to get stuck on
                     such disturbing and seemingly negative control
                     results; not to crush new facts with superficial
                     control; to control the negative control results
                     themselves, and finally not to give in to the urge
                     for comfort that so easily makes one say: "Oh, it
                     was only an illusion." (Reich 1948, pp. 111f)

As for Reich's half-knowledge of physics, these people are certainly partly right, but it was precisely Reich's ignorance, for example, in the field of bacteriology that led him to the discovery of the bions. In exactly the same way, his physical naivety also led him to orgone. The fact is that the physicist who carries out countless measurements in the laboratory every day without ever stumbling upon orgone is, through his entire approach, systematically prevented precisely from letting orgone show itself, just as bacteriologists could work in their laboratories for millennia without ever coming upon pleomorphism. Corresponding samples are disposed of as "contaminated."

www.orgonomie.net
                                          56

Moreover, the matter can be reversed: physicists who, in replicating Reich's experiments, do not adhere exactly to the wording, in particular to the spirit, of his instructions, are — amateurs. I refer in this regard to the work of the Max Planck scientist H.-U. Demisch, with whom Sigusch collaborated. There is not the slightest indication that Demisch ever understood Reich's premises at all. To him, everything about Reich seems too "subjectivistic" and "intuitive." Demisch simply measures metal and styrofoam plates with electronic temperature sensors, without any consideration of environmental influences (weather, ORANUR). Grotesquely, he does not even succeed at the null experiment, i.e., he is constantly confronted with uncontrollable temperature gradients. One tears one's hair out reading his work… (Müschenich 1987).

One can comb through entire libraries, but one will never stumble upon two
things: the orgasm theory and the problem of the superego. These are areas
that have been excised from science out of worldview-related considerations.
Yet the very same people immediately speak of the worldview-based misuse of
science when orgonomy seeks to clarify the difference between the primary and
the secondary. But it is simply a matter of freely following one's inner
(primary!) feelings, rather than an artificial worldview. It is a matter of
making direct contact with reality, unmediated by unfounded opinions, rather
than remaining trapped in ideological illusions. It is about the difference
between contact and contactlessness. Every correct observation must "always
lead to functional, energetic formulations, if one does not

www.orgonomie.net

turn aside beforehand" (Reich 1942, p. 48). "There are certain truths that are
given a priori through our senses and movements" (Reich 1953a, p. 299).

Orgonomy is the immediate, and therefore scientific, expression of reality.
Reich dismissed all metaphysical interpretation, did not follow the
interpretive acrobatics of depth psychology, but instead pursued the tangible
body and its behavior; he investigated the everyday life of objective work
democracy rather than representing political ideologies; he did not follow
teleological prescriptions of purpose or any ends lying outside himself, but
explained the path to the goal. This corresponds to the "Silent Observer," the
sober observer unprejudiced by any worldview (cf. Reich 1953b).


---


While ideologies and religions create worldviews, science discovers: Reich
did not create work democracy as a new subjective worldview, but discovered it
as an objective fact; he did not create a new image of humanity, but discovered
genitality and with it the genital character. There are infinitely many of
those created ideologies and religions, infinitely many worldviews, but only
one single science. One can, according to Reich, hold thousands of opinions
about one and the same thing, but there is only one single correct explanation
for it (Reich 1947, p. 84).

When "Reichians" accuse the orgonomists of "totalitarian orgonomic
fundamentalism" because the latter insist on the scientific foundations,
behind this criticism lies blue fascism (The Blue Fascism,
www.orgonomie.net/hdoblau.htm). These "Reichians" want to cook their various
ideological soups on top of orgonomy. And when they object that they represent
an open, non-totalitarian, tolerant worldview, they are in fact merely agents
of the omnipresent reaction and part of the ostensibly "pluralistic"
suppression of the one scientific truth. Consistently, they skirt what is
essential and marked by the name "Wilhelm Reich": the orgasm theory and the
problem of the superego. One need only think, for example, of how, for reasons
of political correctness, homosexuality is erroneously no longer regarded—no
longer allowed to be regarded—as an illness. One is no longer permitted to
distinguish the secondary from the primary. Orgonomy is supposed to adapt to
the "social consensus," which, interestingly, is always in the mouths of
supposed "progressives."

Reich regarded everything (including God, spirit, self, religion, love, etc.)
as accessible to natural-scientific investigation. For example, orgonomy
resolves philosophical and psychological problems involving consciousness by
extra-philosophical and extra-psychological means. The question of the
unconscious is answered through physiological armoring; the question of the
reality of the external world through the dissolution of ocular armoring. In
this insistence that fundamentally everything is accessible to
natural-scientific research, Reich was far more radical than practically all
other natural scientists, who still leave room outside science for worldviews
and "questions of faith."

Take once again the biophysicist Alfred Gierer, for whom "many different
philosophical, cultural, and religious ideas are compatible with scientific
facts and

www.orgonomie.net

logical thinking. Science yields no binding interpretation of the world; it is
itself in need of interpretation" (Gierer 1991, p. 13).

There is even room for irrational mysticism:


---


Science does not refute religion as such. Science
                       is compatible with the belief that there is no god, one
                       god, or several gods. Man can, but need not, understand
                       the world as God's creation and man as His image.
                       Religion is connected with areas of life that the
                       sciences do not fill and that are nevertheless important

Reich always fought against such "scientific" blank checks for mystical
irrationalism. Already in the 1920s he objected to conceiving of
psychoanalysis as a "bourgeois philosophy of culture," that is, as an
ideology. One is reminded of his brusque rejection of lay analysis, with which
Freud (according to Reich's somewhat skewed assessment) wanted to convert
psychoanalysis from a medical discipline into a worldview. Against this, Reich
set his "science of social sex-economy" (Reich 1946, p. 47). In the chapter on
"The Sex-Economy in the Struggle Against Mysticism," as well as in "Some
Questions of Sexual-Political Practice," one sees that in the struggle against
mysticism (and thereby against the fascist movement) it was always a matter
for him of asserting natural science against mysticism: granting the enemy (!)
no resting places. He asks: "In the struggle between natural science and
mysticism, have all the possibilities of the former been exhausted?" (Reich
1946, p. 161)

Orgonomy is the science that concerns itself with orgone energy and its
functions, that is, fundamentally with everything. It rests on three pillars:
the function of the orgasm, the discovery of the orgone, and the orgonometric
method of thinking. What is common to these three basic elements of orgonomy
is that goals are inherent in them: the orgone as a positive given and its
undisturbed metabolism, that is, sexual, medical, social, and ecological
hygiene. Reality—that is, ultimately the orgonotic current—thus makes
objective demands that have nothing to do with subjective, arbitrary
worldviews. One cannot debate whether there should be hygiene or not; whether
a child should grow up healthy or sick! Self-regulation is self-evident; it is
immanent in the matter itself, whereas the worldviews that are pasted onto
science are always a matter of external determination: the "ought" is imposed
from outside through "revelations"—"superego"!


---


As Reich formulated it during his Marxist period in his critique of
"bourgeois" science, being and ought are not to be separated from one another.
That an ought follows from being is, of course, not to be misunderstood as
meaning that one can simply derive demands from the establishment of facts
(Reich 1945, p. 140), or appeal to the past when making a demand. That,
according to Reich, would be a logical error (Reich 1945, p. 139).

www.orgonomie.net
                                          64

Rather, one must consider the development, identify the regressive and
progressive elements, and suppress or support them accordingly (Reich 1945,
p. 149). So once again it comes down to the contactful distinction between the
secondary, the armored, and the primary, the healthy.23

Reich wanted to bring the supratemporal laws of nature—such as the function of
the orgasm—to bear against the events of the day. This will toward "political"
effectiveness stands in the tradition of the "cosmic politics" of Giordano
Bruno or the "great politics" of Nietzsche (Nietzsche 1889, p. 637f); that is,
it is a matter of reducing history back to nature. For Bruno, it was protest
against the absolutism then taking shape, which did not correspond to the new,
decentralized image of the cosmos; for Bruno this also included the abolition
of belief in a personal God.

In orgonomy, theory and practice are indissolubly linked (again and again:
contact!); that is, there is in it no pure theorizing, no separation between
the humanities and the natural sciences; rather, Reich's social- and
natural-scientific theories always arose directly "on the street" and in the
laboratory, not at the writing desk. Reich's revolutionary pathos, which
appears so unscientific, stems from the fact that, according to him, no
contradiction can be constructed between science and the (organized) pursuit of
social change—that is, the separating and subsequent sequestering of the
secondary from the primary. Yet the separation of being and ought is the
ideology of modern natural science: from factual being, no ideal ought is said
to be derivable; it is said to be decreed only ethically. That this
"scientific" attitude is ideological obfuscation is shown by the
anti-scientific "science business," in which facts are decreed. Lately it is
even voted upon; the "pluralism" of mere opinions triumphs.

In orgonomy, "all boundaries between science and religion, science and art,
objective and subjective, quantity and quality, physics and psychology,
astronomy and religion, God and aether" break down (Reich 1951b, p. 221).


---


Reich says that orgonomy is both a branch of natural science and an artistic procedure (Reich 1951b, p. 221). For example, artistic sensibility predisposes one to the orgonomic observation of nature in CORE work. This does not mean, however, that there must be an "orgonomic" art. On the contrary! Art is something autonomous that is destroyed by any "worldview-related" appropriation. There can be no "orgonomic poems," no "orgonomic pictures," no "orgonomic music," for good art is recognized precisely by the fact that everyone can read something different into it: and since orgonomy is not a worldview, all art—as visible or audible expression of the cosmic orgone energy—is orgonomic.

In reality it was a holistic, "whole-body" thinking that is peculiar to all truly creative and original people, but which Homo normalis cannot distinguish from schizophrenia. 4. Space and the Orgone
4.a. Action at a Distance
"Reichians" no longer live in a world determined by atoms, but in a Cartesian one of "energy movement and vortices," in which a kind of cosmic sauce determines everything and anything. The difference from the mechanistic worldview is only gradual. Cause and effect are explained hydrodynamically rather than mechanically. Both are "theories of local action." There is no place for a simultaneous or nonlocal effect, an "action-at-a-distance theory." The painful gap that remains is then hastily papered over with mystical concepts. Quite similarly, Descartes had to supplement the res extensa, in which everything results from pressure and impact, with an extensionless res cogitans. Two unrelated realms, between which only the unknowable God can mediate.


---


Expressed as tersely as conceivable, the theory of local action says that everything in the world works like a game of billiards, i.e., everything rests on the direct contact between the billiard balls, the cue, and the cushions. An example of an action-at-a-distance theory is Newton's theory of gravitation, which dispenses with any "explanatory mechanism." The fall of the apple from the tree and the motion of the moon around the earth are governed by a hypothetical "force," gravitation, without any mechanism being specified for how this force works. A "mechanism" is also hard to imagine, since the action of "gravity" is supposed to be instantaneous.

Newton himself found this notion deeply repugnant. Yet it was unavoidable. Suppose gravitation propagated at the speed of light: then the earth would be attracted toward the place where the sun was 8 minutes ago, and conversely the sun toward the place where the earth was 8 minutes ago. The result would be that the two objects, in accordance with the laws of gravitation, would spiral toward each other! This consideration was the death of all mechanical local-action theories of gravitation, in particular that of Descartes, who wanted to explain gravitation by means of "ether vortices." He was thinking here of something like a water whirlpool, in which everything moves "gravity-like" toward the center. Newton, too, wanted to trace gravitation back to the ether, just as Descartes had before him, since, as said, the action-at-a-distance theory was personally deeply repugnant to him; he left it at private natural-philosophical speculations, however, which he could not connect with his mathematical formulations.

Einstein solved the problem with his General Theory of Relativity, according to which gravitation is "curved spacetime," and the energy of gravitation can itself "curve spacetime," which cancels out the effect described above, even though gravity propagates only at the speed of light. But when one looks into the vast reaches of the universe with its widely spaced structures, where the speed of light is "maddeningly" slow, every local-action theory, including Einstein's, becomes completely absurd. For then

www.orgonomie.net
                                           67
nothing whatsoever remains that organizes and holds the whole together, i.e., that makes the universe, and even individual galaxies, into a functional unity.24

Something corresponding can be said about the human organism. As F. A. Lange already worked out 150 years ago in his History of Materialism, there is no conceivable step from "atoms," of whatever nature they may be—i.e., the billiard balls of the local-action theory mentioned above—to that which we call "mind" (Lange 1866). I have already worked this out in section 1.d. The mind is something that simply has no "parts." Or put differently: parts constitute an object, but never and in no way a subject. The "mind" represents the unified functioning of the organism.


---


Newton's question of course remains: how one is to understand the action-at-a-distance theory, i.e., what one is actually to imagine by it. As Newton himself said, it is completely absurd. Lange appealed to Kant. The "thing in itself" is unknowable, which is why materialism—otherwise to be preferred in every respect—and with it all human knowledge, reaches its limit here. I have already addressed this, as said, in section 1.d.

As touched upon, the answer is found in Einstein's theory of relativity, according to which space is not a rigid frame as in Newton, in which the billiard game in question unfolds "in time," but rather space and time are interlocked with one another. Depending on the observer—and yet still not merely subjectively—"length contraction" and "time dilation" occur. In the General Theory of Relativity, the running of clocks depends on the curvature of four-dimensional spacetime, in which space and time are united into a kind of "substance" that can theoretically even be shifted at faster-than-light speed (the notorious "warp drive" of science fiction).

When Einstein grappled with the ether problem—first in the Special Theory of Relativity with the "luminiferous ether," then in the General Theory of Relativity with, if one may so speak, the "gravitational ether"—he necessarily encountered the corresponding orgonotic functions, in which, for example, a spatial side-by-side becomes a temporal succession, and correspondingly the separating space seems to disappear. Newton described this for gravity, Henri Bergson for the "mind," which one can describe only "temporally," but not spatially.25

4.b. The Theory of Relativity
The mechanistic conception of nature, resting on a rigid, armored bodily perception, first compelled the postulate of a mechanically conceived ether, to which ever more exotic, i.e., "non-mechanical," properties had to be ascribed in order to remain in agreement with observations. More than three decades before Einstein's Special Theory of Relativity, F. A. Lange noted that many natural scientists would be inclined to abandon the ether entirely (Lange 1866, p. 742).

What remained were abstract formulas and equally abstract postulates no longer grounded in any bodily perceptions, which accordingly could not be brought into any meaningful functional context with other phenomena of nature. In fact, the theory of relativity has proven itself remarkably sterile. One might ask whether it is not owed to it that, in the year 2026, contrary to all predictions, we still do not live in a science-fiction scenario.

Reich writes on the ether problem:


---


The negative outcome of the Michelson-Morley experiment, which sought to demonstrate the ether, must be understood. The premises that led to the conducting of the Michelson-Morley experiment rested on false assumptions. Orgone physics proceeds from entirely new observations and new theoretical assumptions. From a principled orgonomic standpoint, thinking itself must be grasped as a function of nature in general. Accordingly, the results of mere thinking must be regarded as secondary to the observable functions of nature. As functionalists, we are interested first and foremost in observable functions of nature; from there we arrive at the functions of human thought by way of the emotional (bioenergetic) functions in the observing human being. As long as observable nature does not form the starting point for human thinking, and moreover, as long as the function of thinking itself is not derived logically and consistently from the observable natural functions in the observer himself, fundamental methodological and factual questions arise with respect to all results of mere thinking that are not supported by observation. The conclusion that was drawn, on the basis of mere thinking, from the negative outcome of the Michelson experiment shows this clearly. (Reich 1949a, p. 150f)

The radiant force of the living "is present everywhere and fills the whole of space, which has been emptied by empty human beings" (Reich 1953a, p. 268).

www.orgonomie.net
                                          69
In his book on the theory of relativity, Ernst Schmutzer asks:


---


---

description: "Wilhelm Reich Ether Physics and Orgone Experiments - Chunk 67. Source: https://orgonomie.net/hdowrphysik.pdf (German). Author: orgonomie.net. Historical discussion of the ether concept, Huygens wave theory, Michelson-Morley experiment, and Einstein's response."

Where did the ether concept actually come from? (...) Since, according to Huygens' wave theory of light, there are indeed great analogies between light and sound—how dangerous analogies can be, even though they are indispensable for research!—one conceived as a medium for light propagation the ether as an object with strange properties: penetrability of all bodies, imponderability, etc. (...) From these explanations it clearly follows that the mechanics of continua stood godfather to the ether concept. Fundamentally, it was therefore a mechanicism. (...) The spirit of this time, accustomed to the mechanical form of motion, needed—for reasons of a misunderstood intuitiveness, by which was meant a mechanical intuitiveness—the world ether. (Schmutzer 1996, p. 49f)

At the end of the 19th century, Michelson and Morley showed that the Earth could not possibly move through an "ether sea," because the light rays that allegedly moved in wave-like fashion through this sea were not carried along. Astronomical observations had already previously clarified that this "sea" must rest similarly in itself, like the real ocean through which a fast ship (the Earth) glides. To stay with the image: One could detect no (or at most a ridiculously small) movement of the boat relative to the sea, but since the boat did indeed move relative to the land, one could only conclude from this—that the sea does not exist...

The result of the Michelson experiment showed that thinking and experience came into contradiction. In such cases, the facts are decisive; thinking has to align with them. Nature cannot be told how it should be so that one can think in the old familiar way. Thinking has to adapt to nature. In such cases, thought presuppositions often must be analyzed and—if necessary—revised. Upon this, a logically flawless conceptual system can then be grounded (Melcher 1978, p. 237).

This conceptual system and the associated formulas were developed by Einstein.


---


---
name: orgonomie-chunk-68
description: "Wilhelm Reich Ether Physics and Orgone Experiments - Chunk 68. Source: https://orgonomie.net/hdowrphysik.pdf (German). Author: orgonomie.net. Einstein's interpretation of relativity formulas, his approach to light speed, and his 1920 lecture reintroducing the ether concept."
---

The problem is that these formulas must be physically interpreted. For this, it is necessary to find certain "assignment rules" that establish a connection between the mathematical formula symbols and the physical measured quantities. Here lie the actual difficulties hidden, because these assignment rules can be very complex. In the interpretation of the mathematical formulas, errors and differences of opinion can arise. (Sexl, Schmidt 1991, p. 48)

To Leopold Infeld, Einstein once said that "in physics there are actually only a few fundamental ideas, and these can be expressed in ordinary words." He added: "No scientist thinks in formulas" (Herneck 1974, p. 48).

Nevertheless, Einstein always left unexplained "why the speed of light is always equal to c and why one cannot catch up with a light wave. Rather, these experiments must by definition turn out negative, and by definition space is also homogeneous and isotropic for light propagation in every inertial system" (Arthur I. Miller, in: Aichelburg, Sexl 1979).

While physicists like Poincaré wrestled with the concrete physical phenomenon "light" (phase velocity, group velocity, front velocity, flight velocity, one-way light speed, two-way light speed, etc.), Einstein simply spoke abstractly of "light speed." And the physicists, who live from the simplification of complicated problem formulations, followed him like a prophet. When one reads the books of the relativists, one accusation always stands out: that Einstein's predecessors clung too much to physics!

In fact, experimental evidence, such as that of Michelson-Morley, which showed that there is no "ether drift," was meaningless to Einstein; rather, it was mathematical aesthetics, the symmetry of natural laws (a fixed speed of light for every observer), that guided him. From this it is misguided to believe that any experiment could have diverted Einstein from his path.

In 1920, Einstein gave the lecture "Ether and Relativity Theory," in which he reintroduced the ether that he had abolished in 1905 through special relativity. He spoke of the "ether of general relativity theory," an ether with gravitational waves similar to the ether waves of 19th-century physics. Today there are even "gravitational interferometers" that attempt to measure gravitational waves. But this "ether" has nothing to do with orgone, because according to a careful analysis, general relativity excludes the concept of a "substance-like energy" (Schmutzer 1996).


---


Ultimately, Einstein's concept of the universe rested on the reduction of dynamics to kinematics and further to geometry: a universe in which time and chance were mere illusions. That made him the enemy of every "energetics." First, in 1905, he demonstrated the particle nature of matter and light (photons); the general theory of relativity followed. And finally, if he believed in anything, it was in the absoluteness of the second law of thermodynamics, which is already implied by the particle nature of matter alone. Accordingly, it was a labor of unrequited love from the very start to try to get anywhere with Einstein using light phenomena (the orgonoscope) and the thermal disequilibrium between the orgone energy accumulator and its surroundings (cf. Reich 1953c).

That Einstein, with the linking of space and time — as already touched on at the end of the last section — may nonetheless have stumbled upon an orgonotic function, though not in the functional domain of relative motion but

www.orgonomie.net
                                             71

in that of co-existing effect (to which the first radiation [Erstrahlung] also belongs), is at least hinted at by Reich when, in Ether, God and Devil, he attempts to explain the null result of Michelson and Morley not only by the relatively slow movement of orgone energy relative to the Earth's surface, but alternatively also by the first radiation, which is to be differentiated from movement (Reich 1949a, pp. 151f).26

4.c. The cosmic ocean of energy
One cannot simply equate the origins of orgonomy with the "strange cult of the energy concept that prevailed around 1900 and formed the breeding ground for all manner of energetic speculations" (Russelman 1988). What is meant is the epoch of "energetics" of Georg Ferdinand Helm (1951-1932) and Wilhelm Ostwald (1853-1932), as well as the "monism" of Ernst Haeckel.27 Gerald Russelman traces Freud's concept of libido back to this "cult," and from there further back to the beginning of the age of steam engines and the thermodynamics that developed out of that technology (Russelman 1988). Thus Reich's personal experience of a complete discharge of surplus energy through the genital valve coincided with a scientific tradition that culminated in Freud's libido theory. This connection formed the background of Reich's "steam boiler model" of human sexuality.

To this day, orgonomy can invoke the physics concept of energy in this respect without any restriction. Take, for example, the following sentence from the dtv Lexikon für Physik (dtv Encyclopedia of Physics), in which the orgasm theory is already reproduced on a purely physical-mechanical level: "If a physical system contains energy, it can perform work and, in doing so, release energy from its own energy store to the outside." In the energy system of the human being, energy likewise discharges, either by performing work or through the whole-body plasma convulsion at the climax of the genital embrace.


---


Nevertheless, Reich's use of the term "energy" has been attacked again and again, on the grounds that Reich had misused this strictly defined quantity of physics in a "biologistic" way. Compare this with the account of the physicist Nigel Calder:

The concept of energy in physics does not differ very much
                      from its general meaning in everyday life. An
                      "energetic" person works and plays with vehemence
                      and also infects those around her with it. Nations
                      live through "energy crises" (…). For the physicist, energy
                      means the possibility of bringing about changes in the
                      world: making things move (…) making the sun shine (…)
                      making plants grow (…) setting atoms into statistically
                      random motion (heat energy). (Calder 1980)

Reich understood energy in general, and orgone energy in particular, no differently.

But back to Wilhelm Reich's sexual life: if scientists are honest, every single one must probably admit that it was the "ultimate questions" that brought him to the study of the sciences as an adolescent, and that the core of these

4.d. The New Ether

In 1928, George Gamow introduced the "tunnel effect" into physics. It is usually explained by means of the Heisenberg uncertainty relation. According to this, a particle is able to "tunnel through" an inherently impenetrable obstacle because it can evade the law of conservation of energy, provided that, per the time-energy uncertainty, it returns the borrowed energy "in time." One could argue likewise with the position-momentum uncertainty, which permits no resting, energy-free objects with a fixed localization, so that quantum particles in principle cannot be "locked up." In other words: there can be no perfect shielding and therefore no "empty space."29

The argument becomes still more convincing when the uncertainty relation is applied directly to "empty space." If one removes all atoms from a container in order to create a vacuum, thermal radiation remains. But even that cannot be eliminated by cooling to absolute zero, for at absolute zero the "zero-point radiation" is still present — and it would be present even "without walls." For according to the time-energy uncertainty, the strength and rate of change of a field cannot be simultaneously fixed. Since in a field-free vacuum, however, field strength and rate of change would be exactly determined, such a state is in principle impossible. Expressed in terms of quantum statistics, this means that the probability wave can never be entirely eliminated — a residue of probability always remains. Therefore, in quantum mechanics there can be neither a perfect "isolation" nor a vacuum; in every conceivable region of empty space, "vacuum fluctuations" must occur.

Curiously, the new "ether physics" began when quantum mechanics was combined with the Special Theory of Relativity — that is, with the very theory that had banished the ether once and for all. The first step in this direction was taken in 1928 by Paul Dirac, when he reformulated the quantum wave function for electrons relativistically. This modified equation also yielded solutions for "negative energy." Accordingly, an electron could jump to an energy level lying below its zero energy. The atom would thus be just as unstable again as it was before Bohr's atomic model. To prevent this catastrophe, Dirac postulated electrons with negative energy that fill all conceivable negative energy levels and thereby fill all of space. Dirac himself spoke explicitly of the "new ether." What was new about this ether was that, owing to the uncertainty principle applied to it — which prevents the assignment of a definite velocity — it stood, in contrast to the classical stationary ether, in no contradiction to the Special Theory of Relativity.


---


An energy-rich photon can lift a "negative electron" out of this "Dirac sea," by (e.g., colliding with another electron serving as a "catalyst" and) so to speak knocking a "hole" into the vacuum. The hole corresponds to a then still hypothetical "positron" (which was in fact

discovered by Anderson in 1932). When this hole is refilled by the electron, in that electron and positron unite, an identical photon is produced.

In "quantum field theory" (quantum mechanics concerned with interactions), the unobservable Dirac sea into which one could knock "holes" was later abandoned (especially as more and more "elementary particles" were discovered, for each of which separate "seas" would have had to be postulated), but in the conception of quantum field theory the vacuum remains to this day a seething sea, or a "gas," of "virtual particles" that appear "out of nothing" through the pair formation described above and vanish again.

In 1958, M.J. Sparnaay was able to demonstrate the first indications of a measurable effect of these virtual particles, when he tested a quantum effect postulated in 1948 by Hendrick Casimir. Two uncharged metal plates, separated by a very small distance, stand parallel to one another, and are cooled in a perfect vacuum down to absolute zero (both of which, of course, are only approximately attainable). The electromagnetic spectrum associated with the virtual particles (or, expressed differently, the zero-point radiation mentioned above) is restricted between the metal plates, whereby the two plates attract each other, or are pressed toward one another. The force with which this happens is, considering that it comes "out of nothing," considerable.

The American physicist H.E. Puthoff of the Institute for Advanced Studies at Austin states, following a brief description of the quantum vacuum and the various effects attributable to it:

   Thus we see that quantum electrodynamics, which is rooted in

   energetic vacuum that is once again a completely filled space

He even discusses the possibility of tapping this cosmic energy ocean by means of the Casimir effect (Puthoff 1990) — something that Reich had already achieved in practice, by other means, 50 years earlier.

4.e. Cold Fusion and ORUR

In the ORANUR experiment, Reich irradiated radium needles with highly concentrated orgone energy in order to render the radioactive material "harmless." The immediate result was quite the opposite: the orgone energy became "harmful," i.e., over-excited (ORANUR), and finally even died off (DOR). After some years in which the radium needles continued to be exposed to a weak ORANUR reaction, it turned out that they had in fact transformed in roughly the way Reich had envisioned at the beginning of the experiment (Reich 1957).

In the ORANUR experiment (ORgone Against NUklear Radiation), radioactive material had transformed itself in such a way that the NUR reaction of the radium had become an OR reaction: ORUR.

"Cold Fusion" first made a media sensation in 1989. Today it goes by the designation low-energy nuclear reactions (LENR). In 2009, evidence was presented for the first time that neutrons occur in cold fusion (NN 2009). Proof that a nuclear reaction had actually taken place. Apparently the "theoretically impossible" does work after all, and will soon solve our energy problems once and for all.

"Our discovery is very important," said a co-author of the study, the analytical chemist Dr. Pamela Mosier-Boss of the Space and Naval Warfare Systems Center (SPAWAR) of the US Navy in San Diego. "To our knowledge, this is the first scientific report on the generation of high-energy neutrons in a LENR apparatus."

Mosier-Boss and her colleagues inserted nickel and gold electrodes into a palladium dichloride solution mixed with deuterium ("heavy water"). Electric current immediately led to the cold fusion of deuterium atoms. The neutrons released in the process were captured by means of a special plastic, CR-39. After the experiment, tiny clusters of three adjacent pits, each apparently traceable to a common point, could be made out under the microscope in this plastic. They were created when subatomic particles were liberated by the neutrons penetrating the plastic.

www.orgonomie.net
                                              81

occurrence of X-ray radiation, tritium, and excess heat.

It has long been claimed that, with the help of cold fusion, it is also possible to
convert nuclear waste into harmless, non-radiating substances. The "unsolvable
problems" of humanity are — pseudo-problems!

similar to the way chemically contaminated soils are cleaned with the help of
bacteria (Vysotskii, Kornilova 2019).

The physicist Claus Rolfs (University of Bochum) attempted in 2006 (one year before
his retirement) to reproduce nuclear fusion processes inside stars in a particle
accelerator. When he bombarded light atomic nuclei with protons and deuterons, the
fusion rate (that is, the embedding of these particles into the respective atomic
nucleus) was significantly higher when the bombarded nuclei were embedded in metal
rather than in insulating materials, and were cooled to near absolute zero. In
further experiments, Rolfs's group was able to observe a shortened half-life for
radioactive isotopes under the conditions described. They hope that radioactive
waste can be rendered harmless in this way in the future (NN 2006).

The role played in these experiments by the metal (as opposed to "insulating
materials") and by the restriction of "mechanical" thermal motion accords with the
orgone-physical relationships explained here and constitutes a further independent
confirmation of Reich's ORANUR experiment.

Rolfs's explanation is, of course, a different one:

At temperatures near absolute zero, the free electrons [which move freely in
                      the metal like a plasma] approached the atomic nucleus. Their
                      attractive force accelerates positively charged particles — in
                      two directions. On the one hand, they pull the bombardment
                      toward the atomic nucleus, which makes the desired fusion
                      reactions more probable. On the other hand, however, it also
                      seemed possible, according to Rolfs, that positively charged
                      particles could be pulled out of an atomic nucleus by the nearby
                      electrons. This would shorten the half-life in alpha and beta-plus
                      decay.

The fundamental problem of orgonomic research (which applies in very similar fashion
to RET research) is the researchers themselves. Reich found that space energy is not
only an energy "out there," but that it also flows through us. Unfortunately, in our
culture (as in all other patriarchal cultures) this energetic streaming is blocked from
infancy onward (in some parts of the world this goes as far as mutilation of the
sexual organs). Through a contactless (restrictive or permissive) upbringing, people
are bred into a mortal fear of the flow of biological energy. That is why RET and
orgonomy are such hot irons. Tragically, however, the real problems only begin when
these "hot irons" are taken up: people panic in the face of the energetic reality,
"go into their eyes" and speak of "spiritual worlds," "the Grail world," "spiritual
fulfillment," etc. This means nothing other than that one turns to the superego,
respectively to the armor. That is why both the field of RET and that of orgonomy
are so filled with "cranks." One recognizes them by the fact that for them either
"everything is one unity" or the mind is an "independent" entity: the cynical
world-feeling of contactless autists, respectively psychopaths. In a weakened form
one finds this contactlessness when, in a grotesque misreading of Einsteinian
physics, mere mathematical auxiliary notions are suddenly turned into "real"
additional dimensions and "planes of being." From the above exposition, however, it
should have become clear that there is nothing secret, "esoteric," or even
"mystical" about orgonomy.

Does orgonomy offer a unified theory for RET? The answer must be an unambiguous no,
because it is not a theoretical edifice but an approach. An approach which Reich
called "functionalism": it is not a matter of erecting models over nature
("substitute contact"), but of adapting one's own thinking to nature. Because this
failed to happen in science, the fundamentally simple RET and orgonomy slipped
through the meshes of science. What is needed, therefore, is not new model
conceptions, which would only block the view again, but a radically new way of
seeing, whose leitmotif is the search for "common functional principles," as I have
attempted here in the comparison of RET and orgonomy.

5. The Atom and the Orgone
5.a. Transubstantiation
Galileo Galilei was persecuted not because he championed the heliocentric world
view, but because he championed the atomistic one. From a theological point of view
it was ultimately of secondary importance whether the sun circles the earth or vice
versa. The composition of matter, by contrast, was anything but a matter of
indifference. For if matter is composed of atoms, there is no place whatsoever for
transubstantiation, i.e. the essential transformation of bread and wine into the
flesh and blood of Christ during the Eucharist. Atoms are immutable, fixed units
which can indeed combine into the most diverse objects, but their essence is thereby
fixed. "Bread is bread" and "wine is wine." In place of God and His action matter and
its autonomous laws enter. For example, following these laws, bread can go moldy,
wine can evaporate, etc. In other words: Galileo stood for materialism. God was to
shatter, as it were, against the atoms!

Materialism means that not only everything around us, but also we ourselves, are
nothing but essence-less objects composed, so to speak, of "Lego bricks" (atoms). As
already set out in section 1.d., this world view has a great problem: it offers no
conceivable explanation for our subjective inner life, our mental functions, our
sense perceptions, our emotions. And it is precisely here that the "discovery of the
orgone" by the avowed materialist Reich sets in. To be sure, in his "bioelectrical
experiments" of the mid-1930s he made the emotions visible, but the measurement
results eluded all materialist explanations. He observed something similar in the
immediately ensuing "bion experiments," which were about "destroying" matter and
letting it reorganize itself. From this research sprang the development of the
orgone energy accumulator and, finally, the DOR-buster, with whose help matter can
be charged, respectively discharged, with an energy that is "immaterial" (i.e. not
composed of atoms or quantum objects) and yet tangible. This "orgone energy" was the
specific energy of the emotions.


---


Materialism finally broke apart in the ORANUR experiment, when it turned out
that atoms can not only decay radioactively, as had been discovered decades
earlier, but that matter can transform itself independently of its atomic
structure. Reich called this "pre-atomic chemistry."

What is that supposed to be? Let us widen the lens a bit: Antarctic dry valleys are
deserts hostile to life in every respect, yet in spring their sandy soils are often
strewn with moist spots despite the absence of snowmelt and
rain. A study by geologist Joseph Levy of Oregon State University has
shown that these spots are created by salt deposits that suck moisture out
of the atmosphere. For the effect, a combination of the right salts and
sufficient moisture in the atmosphere is necessary. With sodium chloride
(table salt), the process requires 75 percent humidity; with
calcium chloride, only 35 percent (NN 2012a).

www.orgonomie.net
                                        90

The salts are blown in from the sea, derive from ancient fjords that once
flooded the area, and, according to Levy, also from snowflakes (or rather
naturally from the condensation nuclei they contain). The soil spots that form in
this way are three to five times richer in water than their surroundings. Moreover, they
are full of organic matter, including living microbes.

According to Levy, his team discovered this process in the course of a "geology
while walking," i.e. they noticed the mysterious spots in the Antarctic
desert soil and then set about searching for the cause of their
occurrence. Levy on the formation process finally discovered by
process of elimination:

It seems somehow peculiar, but it really
                    does work. Before one of our trips, I placed a bowl with
                    the dried-out, salty soil and a glass of water
                    in a sealed Tupperware container and left the whole
                    thing on my bookshelf. When I came back, the
                    water had moved from the glass to the salt and had
                    formed a salt brine there. I knew that this would happen, but
                    somehow I was still surprised.

I had a solution of calcium chloride standing in my apartment for years. It
simply would not dry out. It was a small miracle every time I took
the bowl down from the shelf.

What does this have to do with orgonomy? After all, what is at stake here is the possible formation
of oases of life in arid regions, and secondly, the whole thing recalls
"orene."

Orene (I hate the Germanized "Orin") is, so to speak, the "material form" of
orgone energy, which Reich discovered at the end of 1953. For example, snow is not
simply "crystalline water," but a form of orene. In winter one
senses that dry snow gives off a strong effect — as the English puts it so
nicely, an "uplifting" one — which is lost the instant the snow becomes damp.


---


The "pre-atomic" substance orene (Oe) is an intermediate step between
mass-free orgone energy (OR) and matter (M), and accordingly shows
qualities of both life and of "dead, mechanical matter."

www.orgonomie.net
                                         91

In the laboratory, orene is produced by exposing solutions of caustic soda (sodium hydroxide) or
ordinary table salt in common clay bowls to the atmosphere.
Over time, a white substance deposits on the liquid and on the rim of the clay bowl —
orene. Orene forms spontaneously in our homes.
It is the white substance found on the clay pellets of hydroponics
after a "salt brine" has formed through evaporation.

Here we have arrived directly at "ORANUR chemistry." It was a
side stage of Reich's work with the cloudbuster, with which he removed DOR from
the atmosphere in the aftermath of the ORANUR experiment. From the DOR-
contaminated atmosphere, the powdery substance melanor (Melan-OR,
"black orgone") settled out. Today this effect is known to environmental agencies as
the inexplicable "fogging phenomenon." It is reminiscent of the toner of
photocopiers and is found, e.g., on the walls behind old tube televisions and PC
monitors. Further products of this atmospheric ORANUR chemistry
are brownite and orite, brownite being basically just a mixture of
melanor and orite. Orite, in turn, is the dead form of orene, which
represents, so to speak, "materialized orgone energy." Orene, so to speak, "eats up" the DOR
and melanor. But when it dries out, it itself becomes an energetically
toxic substance — the aforementioned orite.

In my view, the Plocher technology points in a direction similar to ORANUR
chemistry; in it, for example, the immaterial information "oxygen"
is applied to quartz sand, so that the quartz sand actually acts like an oxygen source.

5.b. Seriality
In his book Wilhelm Reich, Biologist, the science historian
James Strick, who specializes in biology, sets out, among other things, that Reich's "obsession" with the
concept of "energy" or "life energy" must be understood against the background of
the disputes between vitalism and mechanism in the early 20th
century, and was merely consistent. Thus, according to Strick, "life
energy" at that time was not a "vitalist" concept but one of
laboratory science. Likewise, for example, as early as 1895, Freud's then
comrade-in-arms Josef Breuer argued that the term "life force" was antiquated and
that one should now (i.e. in 1895, when psychoanalysis was in its earliest beginnings) speak
of "energy" (Strick 2015).


---


More recently it has been proposed that a better conceptual vocabulary, one better adapted to the scientific
terminology in common use today, would be the word
"plasma." "A physicist would perhaps call orgone a plasma" (Jörgenson
1990, p. 104). Lars Jörgenson, in his book on life-energy
concepts in the "gray area of science," accordingly speaks in one place of "orgone plasma"
instead of orgone energy (Jörgenson 1990, p. 149). So are we dealing with a gas
permeated by ionized particles? James DeMeo has spoken of the "(proto)plasmatic
cosmic life energy." The expression in parentheses is what matters most in this
context. After all, there is also, in Ether, God and Devil, with
its reference back to Democritus's "soul atoms," which Democritus imagined as fire atoms,
something like an "orgonomic plasma concept" (Jörgenson 1990, p.
82). In this connection I refer to Reich's statement: "The famous
German physiologist Pflüger suspected a relationship of life energy to
fire on the basis of the function of cyan. His conjecture was correct" (cf. Reich
1948, p. 32).

Commonly, the question of what "orgone energy" is, is frequently followed by the answer that
orgone energy is the "ether," a "substance" that fills space like a gas,
corresponding to the "ether" concept of the vulgar-mechanistic physics of the 19th
century. But this convenient traditional approach, as represented, e.g., by
Courtney F. Baker (Baker CF 1987, pp. 6-12), makes orgonomy
more mechanistic than modern physics. C.F. Baker's whole approach
is astonishingly reminiscent of the 19th century, with book titles like "The Force. A
realistic-monistic worldview. Vol. I: Contraction energy, the ultimate-unitary
mechanical mode of action of the world-substrate" by J.G. Vogt (Leipzig
1878) (Colli, Montinari 1988, p. 647).

Reich, however, was very careful not to equate orgone energy with the "ether" of
classical physics (Reich 1949a, pp. 139-142). While the "ether,"
according to Reich, is the core concept of mechanism, "God" is that of mysticism (Reich
1949a, p. 40 and p. 51). It should be more than obvious that
orgone energy can no more be identical with the mechanistic concept of the "ether"
than with the mystical concept "God." Whoever affirms the one
must also accept the other. That the duality of "ether" and "God" (even
if one is imagined with the supposed exclusion of the other)

out on the basis of hypotheses and theories" (Hunger 1964, p. 38). The perception of
one's own pulsatile functioning is the corresponding "theoretical" foundation that
cannot be questioned further, upon which orgone physics must be built.40

5.c. The orgonomic potential and the living
By the roundabout route of the supposedly "paradigm-shattering" quantum
mechanics, mechanistic physics broke into the realm of the living. In 1929, the
physics professor Orso Mario Corbino delivered a programmatic address on "The
new aims of experimental physics," in which he demanded a fusion of the "new
physics" — meaning quantum mechanics — with biology into a new molecular
biology. For the "greatest benefit of science," Hermann von Helmholtz had given up
his chair of physiology in order to teach physics (compare Section 2.b.). Today, he
said, matters lay exactly the other way around. Now physics had "all the tools at
hand to help other, less advanced sciences along" (quoted in Segrè 1984).

And indeed, from the mid-1930s onward, "life research" was carried to a considerable
extent by people who had begun their careers as physicists. Max Delbrück is to be
named first here — a student of Max Born, who with his "probability interpretation"
of quantum mechanics can be regarded as the actual founder of the "new physics."
What Delbrück wrought in biology can be gauged by what he made of "entelechy":
Delbrück equated the "program" of DNA with Aristotle's concept of the soul.
According to this, the "eidos" (the "unmoved mover") "offers a perfect description of
DNA: it acts, creates form and development, and yet is itself subject to no change
whatsoever in the process." What was once mystical "teleology" in vitalism became
"teleonomy" in Delbrück's molecular biology: "processes (behaviors) whose
goal-directedness is controlled by a program" (Mayr 1979).42


---


Material DNA became, in this way, the replacement for the old (mystically distorted) notion of a special, non-material "life force." What was new in this quantum-mechanically influenced biology, compared to older purely mechanistic designs, was that organisms were no longer conceived as mere "clockworks" that follow mechanical laws "teleomatically." This term designates processes "which reach a final state that is dictated by natural laws, but not by a program" (Mayr 1979). The said "final state" is, of course, maximum entropy, and ultimately the heat death of the universe.

If one now re-wraps the matter using the orgonomic potential as a guide, one recognizes where the whole problem is buried: In biologically organized nature, one had to find something that corresponded to the passive reaching of a "final state," yet yielded results that corresponded to the active striving toward an "ideal purpose." The notion of a "program goal" made this conceptual union of materialism and vitalism possible. Delbrück and his colleagues struggled in this way with a problem that, thanks to the simultaneous discovery of orgone energy, did not exist at all. That is precisely the contrast between mechano-mysticism and functionalism!

Our entire approach to nature is fundamentally botched. One scientist once said that, if Reich were right, "all of us" would have to be crazy.

That is why the "facts" are a problem. Reich put forward the treatment of newborns as an example. For decades, the "experts" simply ignored their screaming and wailing, treated them like objects, separated them from the mother after birth, denied them nourishment when they were hungry, cut off their foreskins without anesthesia, etc. The "experts" were simply deaf and blind to the suffering of the living. And this example can be transferred, without gaps, to all areas of science.

5.e. Beyond Consciousness
In what follows, we want to make the dissolution of the separation between researcher and object of research concrete using the determination of being "movement." To that end, we turn to Zeno's paradox, the beginning of all philosophy in the 6th century BC: If I try to imagine an arrow in flight, it remains, at the moment of my contemplation, frozen in the space of imagination. But if the arrow is at rest in every moment, it can surely not possibly move.

According to the laws of formal logic, "being here" and "being there" on the one hand, and the "being nowhere" of movement on the other, are mutually exclusive. From the assertion of this contradiction, Platonic philosophy developed—the separation between the world of absolute being (subject) and the illusory world of becoming and passing away (object). Only Hegel and quantum physics, after more than 2000 years, should dissolve this nonsensical separation again, by regarding the being of spirit itself as movement.44

Classical dialectic formed around Zeno's unsolved paradox. Hegel created his dialectic by resolving the paradox. In his Lectures on the History of Philosophy he says: "That dialectic first occurred to us in connection with movement is precisely the reason that dialectic is itself this movement, or that movement itself is the dialectic of all that is."

With this, Hegel wants to say that spirit, which entangles itself in a contradiction when it attempts to grasp movement as something that is, at the same time finds itself in objective movement outside itself, as soon as it overcomes formal logic. Thus it recognizes that movement is "the concept of the true soul of the world"; "we are accustomed to regard it as a predicate, a state, but it is in fact the self, the subject as subject, the persisting precisely of that which vanishes." Through the contemplation of movement, and through the resolution of the contradiction that arises from this contemplation, spirit becomes aware that it is itself one with movement, the "true soul of the world." Spirit accomplishes this by resolving the contradiction of thesis and antithesis in the synthesis. But it can do this only because this dialectic is the essence of movement itself. Reich expressed almost exactly the same thing orgonometrically as follows:


---

'going-beyond-oneself,' which, according to modern analyses, fits well with the
ontological challenges of quantum field theory."

www.orgonomie.net

I underlined the "almost" here because Hegel, apart from mere rhetoric, was fundamentally only capable of reaching concepts. As Jacob Meyerowitz demonstrated in a corresponding orgonometric analysis, Hegel was caught in the laws of pure thought-activity and did not fit his thinking to the functional developmental direction of nature (Meyerowitz 1994). So, since even Hegel ultimately violated nature, he could not really resolve Zeno's paradox completely. Only orgonometry can accomplish that.

Within orgonomy, Hegelian dialectic can claim a restricted validity for itself, since it contains the triadic thought structure and the functional identity of subject and object. Something similar holds for quantum physics, because it lets the world unfold in continual bifurcations and because it has overcome the separation between observer and observed object.

To be able to comprehend this, we must consider the double-slit experiment and ascertain the behavior of several electrons flying individually through the experimental apparatus. In doing so, we see that each individual electron behaves like a continuous wave interfering with itself, flying through both slits simultaneously. But if we are curious and check through which of the two slits the fundamentally indivisible (sic!) electron passes, the electron behaves, entirely in accordance with our approach, like a discontinuous particle and forms no interference pattern. In effect, this is Zeno's paradox transferred from the space of imagination into physical space: observation makes flowing, interlocking functioning congeal. The quantum physicist speaks here of the "collapse of the wave function" through observation.

This common interpretation of quantum mechanics is called the "Copenhagen interpretation." It leads to a paradox, because the world depends on our observation, so nothing is really real. But so that we don't land in the loony bin, we can think up an alternative interpretation of quantum mechanics in which everything is real and which contains no paradox whatsoever. This is the "many-worlds interpretation of quantum mechanics." According to it, the wave function does not collapse at all, but rather splits each time into two "mutually orthogonal worlds." Each individual quantum event thus leads to the bifurcation of the entire universe. Accordingly, there are at least umpteen-quarzintillions of versions of this book! So much for landing in the loony bin…

www.orgonomie.net
                                         106


---


Both interpretations of quantum mechanics have in common that the subject is free: we bring the wave function to collapse, or it is we who choose our path through the branchings of the many worlds. Both thus ultimately imply that we create our own reality. And precisely this metaphysical freedom of the subject generates the positively psychotic bizarreness of these two orthodox interpretations. It is as if modern man, cut off from his roots, had transferred his experience of the world into physics.

But can the two recognized interpretations of quantum mechanics even be justified in view of the already-mentioned nonlocal EPR phenomenon, which demonstrated the incredibly important role that the common functioning principle (CFP) plays in the quantum world? Would it not be in better agreement with physical reality to assert that both my decision to observe something and the collapsing of the wave function have a common cause? Or, expressed better: observer and observed object are superficial variations of the autonomous functioning of orgone energy taking place at a deeper level, in the sense of "coexistent effect," which I will address in chapter 6.

This interpretation allows us to fit quantum mechanics into orgonomic functionalism, as it was described by Reich in Ether, God and Devil. In the book, Reich writes the following about the mathematized world of the quantum physicists:

5.f. Particle and Wave
Einstein was a critic of quantum mechanics from the very beginning, since it threatened to shatter the unity of physics. How, for example, can light be an electromagnetic wave and simultaneously a particle (the "photon" discovered by Einstein)? What light is at a given moment is determined, so quantum mechanics says, by the observer—or naturally by his experimental approach! Nothing like that occurs in the physics of the visible, and so it should not appear in the world of the invisibly small either. Just as Kant, with his "thing-in-itself," had once again given religion and ethics some room to maneuver, quantum mechanics opened the gates to "spirituality." As a consequence, we are meanwhile drowning in the obscurantist swill of the "Tao of Physics."

The arguments that Einstein advanced against Niels Bohr and others became more and more complicated over time and were based on the fact that, if one takes quantum mechanics seriously, the world of the subatomic is bizarre and ghostly. His reflections finally culminated in the already-mentioned EPR paradox, which states that two spatially separated particles can interact instantaneously (that is, as if there were no space between them).45 For the quantum mechanicians, such objections were merely confirmation of their own arguments: that on the subatomic level, after all, different laws applied, and that the physical laws of our macroscopic environment were merely limiting cases of the far more fundamental quantum mechanics—similar to how mechanics is a limiting case of Einstein's relativity theory.


---


Finally, Einstein fell back on the simplest conceivable counterargument: he simply pointed at the moon! The moon is immovable, locatable in space and time with any desired precision, and is utterly indifferent to whether and how it is observed by any human being or not.

Let us follow Einstein and now consider a subatomic particle that one can imagine as similar to the moon: a proton. A proton orbited by an electron yields the simplest and lightest of the elements, the hydrogen atom.

One of the most fundamental properties of this proton is its radius. And that is precisely where the problem begins, for while one can measure the radius of the moon with arbitrary precision, the problems pile up with the proton. And that even setting aside Heisenberg's famous "uncertainty relation."

One can estimate the size of the proton by bombarding it with electrons and measuring their deflection. Far more precise are indirect measurements with the help of the so-called "Lamb shift." The Lamb shift is the splitting of an energy level that the electron in a hydrogen atom can occupy. This extremely small splitting could be explained with quantum electrodynamics, which describes the interaction of protons, electrons, and photons—and in doing so turns the vacuum into a bubbling foam of virtual elementary particles.

Hydrogen atoms are bombarded with lasers of a certain frequency; if one has hit upon the right frequency, the Lamb shift occurs, which the atom announces by emitting a corresponding photon. From the frequency of the laser radiation one infers the magnitude of the Lamb shift, and from there, with inconceivably complicated calculations, the "radius of the proton" (more precisely, the "charge radius")—which in this fashion is in truth no longer anything more than an interpretation-requiring quantity in a barely transparent abstract formula.


---


In 2010, preliminary measurement results seemed to indicate that up to then one had miscalculated by a whopping 4 percent in the "radius of the proton" (Pollmann 2010). Yet elementary particle physicists have investigated nothing more precisely than protons! Everything we look at consists exclusively of protons, neutrons, and electrons. It would really be just as if an astronomer today were to determine that the moon is actually 4 percent smaller than previously assumed. What would then become of the tides, the moon landings—fundamentally, of the whole of astronomy, if not even of physics?!

That will never happen with the moon, but with the proton this case could actually occur if the results should be confirmed. The measurement of the proton radius could be improved by a factor of 10 by replacing the stable electron with an extremely short-lived muon, which is 200 times larger than an electron. The "muonic hydrogen atom" accordingly exhibits a larger, more measurable Lamb shift.

And two years later, these results were indeed confirmed:

                     behind them. (NN 2013)

A further 10 years later, the "smaller proton diameter" was confirmed through new measurement methods and highly complicated statistical calculations (NN 2023).

It simply leads nowhere to regard a proton as a "small moon" (or, more fittingly, as a "small sun")—if only because a moon 4 percent smaller would truly knock the world, or rather our world, off its hinges, while protons 4 percent smaller merely throw into disarray a few theories that were baseless anyway (the "standard model").

At the beginning, then, stands the "shaking-up"—an anentropic act at whose end stands maximal entropy, which is then gradually broken down.
