Bernd Laska: The Encounter Wilhelm                                 80


---


Reich – Albert Einstein (1976)
Preliminary remark on the problem of recognition
Reich encountered bitter opposition to his theories from the very beginning—whether among the "enlightened" psychoanalysts; whether among the Communists and Socialists with their "scientific worldview"; or whether among the "sober and matter-of-fact" natural scientists—to say nothing of other groups. Each of these three groups, which flattered itself on its rationality, "liquidated" Reich in its own way, but none seriously engaged with his arguments.

Now, Reich never became, in the proper sense, an unpolitical person who would have been satisfied with his research and a small following. He had merely come to the view that the changing of the world for the better could no longer be achieved through relatively undirected activity and agitation, even when this runs under the (never clearly filled-out in content) catchwords "progress," "freedom," "emancipation," etc.; a problem that is unfortunately still as current today as it was forty years ago, when Reich detached himself from organized Marxism.

And in accordance with this insight—which Reich did not acquire through philosophical speculations and wishful thinking, but through practical and theoretical work on humanity and society—Reich worked on a scientific image of the human being that was to grasp the whole person and his position in nature and society, and that could give a concrete sense to the catchwords mentioned above.

I believe that the origin of the orgone research, often described as unpolitical, is not fully comprehensible without this perspective. After the fiasco in the Soviet Union, Reich did not resign like so many former sympathizers and "fellow travelers" from the West; rather, he sought and found the cause—which, however, lies very much deeper than the ordinary intellectual would like to accept. The topic tempts one to further elaboration; but I must break off here within the scope of this preliminary remark. Only this much more: When Reich later strove, for example, to develop an orgone motor, it was surely not in order to compete with conventional motors. With it he wanted both to broaden the scientific foundation of his discoveries (thus also of the function of the orgasm), and to bring people to engage with it. He, of all people, will have been fully aware that this alone would long since not have sufficed; but it could have been a beginning.

Similar motivations can be attributed to many of his activities in orgone research—whether it was the experiments on the cancer problem, on immunization

80
     Reprinted with the kind permission of the author. From: Wilhelm Reich Blätter 2/76N.

www.orgonomie.net
                                        190


---


against radioactivity (Oranur), or on weather modification. In a higher (or deeper) sense, orgone research was always highly political (at least intentionally), because it was never to be separated from Reich's person and his image of humanity, which could first of all give many "left" ideals a scientific foundation, and thereby rid them of their illusionistic character.

The establishing of contact with Einstein, as a world-famous authority, must unconditionally be seen against the background of the above considerations. Naturally it also plays a role that Reich was not a physicist, and that the abundance of his discoveries had grown over his head alone. In essence, however, it was a renewed attempt to break through his "lone-fighter existence."

What is said in Ilse Ollendorff's Reich biography about the "Einstein affair" I do not wish to repeat here in detail (Ollendorff, Reich 1975), but rather to speak more about the factual details as they emerge from The Einstein Affair (Reich 1953c).

Reich's meeting with Einstein took place on January 13, 1941, and lasted five hours. Einstein was very impressed, and described the phenomenon of the temperature difference between the accumulator and the surrounding air as a "bomb" for physics, if it could be established flawlessly. He carried out measurements himself, and arrived at results similar to Reich's. In his letter of February 7, 1941, however, he stated: "…one of my assistants has now drawn my attention to the fact that in rooms… the floor temperature is always lower than the temperature at the ceiling. If a horizontal fixed plate is present (the tabletop on which the accumulator stood during the experiment—B.L.), then the underside of the plate communicates more with the floor through convection, the upper side more with the ceiling… This circumstance has finally turned out to be the decisive one… In light of these experiments I consider the matter completely cleared up" (Reich 1953c).

Although Reich already knew the problem of convection from his earlier experiments, and had already eliminated this influence, he carried out a series of different experiments before writing Einstein a long reply letter on February 20, 1941. The first part of the letter consists of a description of the experiments, together with the measurement results and a summarizing treatise. Reich had carried out the following experiments:

1. in the room
          1.1 Arrangement of the room thermometer likewise above the tabletop (with
          Einstein it hung freely in the room next to the tabletop).
          1.2 Attachment of a second wooden board about 1 m above the tabletop on
          which the accumulator stood, to interrupt the convection effect from
          the ceiling.
          1.3 Replacement of the wooden tabletop by a metal plate (a better
          heat conductor).
          1.4 Accumulator hanging freely in the room, without a table.
          In all cases the temperature difference between the interior of the
          accumulator and the surrounding air resulted.

www.orgonomie.net
                                         191


---


2. in the open air
          Here an even stronger temperature difference resulted, because the
          influences of walls, tables, etc., were absent.
          The second part of the letter consisted of a detailed description
          of the biophysical context of the temperature phenomenon, in order to
          counteract the impression that this stood isolated and more by chance
          for discussion.

To this very factual letter of Reich's, Einstein never replied. At first Reich believed that Einstein would make further control experiments, to be quite sure. On September 23, 1941, Reich reported in a letter to Einstein, supplementarily, on measurements he had carried out in the summer regarding the discharge speed of the electroscope in dependence on weather changes. At the end he hinted discreetly that he was waiting for a message from Einstein concerning the temperature difference. After all, he had already been without an answer for more than half a year. But Einstein reacted no more.

There was only one further exchange of letters between the secretaries of the two men, about the handling of the return of orgone devices that were still with Einstein. Not until February 1944 did the editor of Reich's International Journal of Sex-Economy and Orgone Research, Dr. Wolfe, write to Einstein that, on the basis of rumors that Einstein could not confirm the experimental results, he considered it necessary to publish the affair. Thereupon Einstein threatened legal steps in the event that his name were brought into any connection with the orgone matter. He did not wish to see his name used for advertising purposes, especially not for a cause that he could not endorse. To this insulting insinuation, Reich wrote a letter of protest to Einstein, also in the name of all his coworkers. Thereupon Einstein replied once more briefly. Only now did he write that three years earlier he had not reacted to several letters from Reich because he had formed a judgment as best he could, and no longer wanted to spend time on the matter. He asked Reich, moreover, to treat all his oral and written utterances discreetly, which he, conversely, had always done too. With that, the "Einstein affair" ended (Reich 1953c)!


---


For a long time Reich was not clear about why Einstein might have withdrawn in such a way, when he had initially shown great understanding and interest, and had also cooperated. Among his coworkers there was a series of theories about it, all of which were naturally unverifiable. When, then, on March 17, 1950, a report appeared in the New York Times according to which Einstein's former assistant Leopold Infeld—born in Poland and emigrated in 1936—was returning to his homeland, Reich believed the riddle of Einstein's behavior solved: as part of a communist-directed campaign against him. This was by no means a delusional idea out of the blue. Anyone who has read Reich's document collection Conspiracy on this complex (Reich 1954a), and is aware that what is documentable may be only the tip of an iceberg, will not find this classification of the Infeld case so strange. That Infeld was a Marxist already follows from the fact that he went to Stalinist Poland; but also in his autobiography Life with Einstein he describes himself as an "undogmatic Marxist," who learned of the deeds of the Stalin era only after the XXth Congress of the CPSU (Infeld 1969, pp. 194 and 170). Admittedly, after being

www.orgonomie.net
                                          192

Einstein's assistant from 1936-38, he taught mathematics at the University of Toronto until the end of the Second World War, and ("as far as I remember") did not see Einstein during that time.

The matter was never further cleared up. One should perhaps try to imagine the atmosphere among America's physicists (many of them German emigrants) when Otto Hahn in Germany achieved the first uranium nuclear fission. Einstein too—who admittedly did not belong to the inner circle of physicists of the (atomic bomb) "Manhattan" project—was very much engaged here. Moreover, he was theoretically isolated from the young generation of physicists, because he refused to fully recognize the quantum mechanics founded by Heisenberg and others ("God does not play dice!"). He was working on a theory that built on his original one and was supposed to be superior to quantum mechanics. In this situation, the Reich matter surely came at an inconvenient time for him, especially since Reich's theory, as later turned out, required the assumption of an ether. Einstein may have suspected this consequence, and therefore withdrawn, for his life's work rested, after all, on the abolition of the ether concept (Clark 1974).

However it may have been—whether a communist-directed campaign, whether an individual assistant with Marxist or otherwise hostile inclinations, whether rumors from psychoanalytic circles, or whether it was Einstein's own decision—what remains important for us is:

1. Einstein described the temperature difference between the accumulator
          and the surrounding air as a "bomb" for physics.

2. Einstein confirmed the temperature difference through his own
          measurements, but then interpreted it in a conventional way.


---


3. Reich carried out further experiments, eliminating the possibility of
          convection, and achieved the same effect—which appeared even more
          distinctly in the open air—and communicated this to Einstein.

4. Einstein pulled out of the affair in a strange way.

The temperature difference was later confirmed by Reich and others countless times, and was the basis for Reich's further work. But Reich's works were never again taken seriously or checked by established science.

www.orgonomie.net
                                            193

Bernd Laska: Orgonomic form and                81

spiral wave (1976)
In his book Cosmic Superimposition, the parts of which of interest here are now also available in German translation in the just-published Selected Writings (Reich 1976), Reich attempts to describe fundamental geometric figures that are observable in many natural processes. He proceeds, among other things, from two experimental observations:

1) In the darkened orgone room—i.e., a room of about house-room size lined on all sides with sheet steel—one can, after some time of acclimatization, observe with the naked eye glowing orgone energy units that move through the room along a helical line (Reich called it the spiral wave, KRW). (Reich attaches to this some hypothetical considerations on the origin of inert mass from the mass-free orgone energy ocean, which, however, need not concern us further here (Reich 1976, p. 322f).)

2) From observations in Experiment XX, Reich arrives, generalizing, at the conclusion that there is a basic form of the living that does not coincide with any known form of classical geometry (Reich 1976, p. 342)—the orgonomic form, as he calls it. In its purest expression, this form is represented by the hen's egg (Reich 1976, p. 345). "All the forms of the realm of life can be traced back to the egg form without constraint" (Reich 1976, p. 344).

Without going into the various geometric (Reich writes, oddly, also "trigonometric") connections between the egg form and the spiral wave, which Reich attempts to establish in this section of his book, I would like here only to state that for the most part they appeared to me difficult to downright impossible to follow.


---


In connection with these expositions on fundamental forms, Reich makes further more or less hypothetical statements about macroscopic processes, such as the origin of whirlwinds and even the formation of galactic structures. For this reason it seems to me interesting to point out cross-connections to the theories of another group of scientific outsiders, which build on the findings of Viktor Schauberger, who died in 1958. In these theories, the function of the rolling-up spiral movement (vortex) occupies a central position. Although a number of statements that are mutually compatible exist between Schauberger's theory and physical orgonomy, this is not to be pursued further at this point. A detailed presentation is planned for a later edition of the WRB. Especially interesting for our present considerations is the formulaic capture of the egg form published in issues V/2 and VI/1-4 of the journal Kosmische Evolution of the Schauberger group, which I have so far found in no mathematics book, as well as its geometric derivation from the form of the (vortex) funnel. The space curve of the spiral wave, so named by Reich, is in turn likewise represented on

81
     Reprinted with the kind permission of the author. From: Wilhelm Reich Blätter 6/76.

www.orgonomie.net
                                        194

this funnel—namely, as the projection of the plane harmonic spiral onto its lateral surface.

www.orgonomie.net
                                         195

For those who have a deeper interest in investigations of this kind, I would like to give a few further pointers:

www.orgonomie.net
                                         196

1) If one derives the egg form from the hyperbolic funnel, for example, not in such a way that one forms the angle Α through the connection of points 1 and 2 of the unit hyperbola (octave), but chooses a different numerical ratio, one obtains a different orgonomic form—for example, that of the rye grain with a larger Α.

2) On the hyperbolic funnel shown, a series of mathematical relations can be established which, among other things, also interpret many laws of the theory of harmony geometrically (cf. on this the theories of Kayser and Rudolf Haase).

3) In connection with the mathematical laws of harmony that can be represented on the geometric figures mentioned, it is to be considered whether the projection of the spiral wave onto the base plane of the hyperbolic funnel must really be the harmonic spiral, or rather a clothoid. This is a curve which, like the circle, is determined by only one parameter A. Its law of formation then reads R × L = A (see also sketch). For, according to a report in the Soviet journal Biofisika XIX/2, pp. 364f (1974), the exact surveying of the spiral structure of the inner ear (where the sensory cells lie) of humans and some mammals showed that these all have the form of the clothoid.


---


Although at the moment I do not see to what extent the connections presented and indicated can contribute to a deeper understanding of the natural functions described by Reich, I nevertheless considered them worth communicating and interesting for the readers of the WRB.

orgone motor? State of the art
             82

(2017)
This paper is the first of a series of three papers focusing on the Reichian orgone motor, appearing in the Journal of Psychiatric Orgone Therapy (http://www.psychorgone.com).83 The present one is a brief report on the state of the art. The second paper (co-author Dionisio Ferrari) concentrates on laboratory experiments carried out by the authors themselves, in which a spontaneous cyclical production of electrical energy from orgone apparatuses was obtained in an average orgonotic environment. The third paper (co-author Dionisio Ferrari) reports and discusses the reaction of the orgone apparatuses in the production of electrical energy when the orgonomic potential of the environment is artificially increased. A hypothesis for the Y factor is developed and described in the last paper.

Reich spent his whole life studying and researching a cosmic energy omnipresent in nature, whose use would have had dramatic social and technological effects on people's lives. Toward the end of the year 1950, after many years of researching the biological and physical properties of this cosmic energy, which he called orgone energy, Reich began using a Geiger-Müller counter to investigate the interaction between orgone energy and radioactivity.84 He thought that orgone energy could weaken, or even annul, the dangerous and harmful effect of radioactivity on organisms and life forms in general. He planned and carried out this kind of research partly because of the emerging Korean War, in order to find an answer and an antidote to the threat of a possible nuclear conflict. He was convinced that:

Nuclear radiation, e.g., radium, is altered under the
                      influence of concentrated orgone energy. The kind and
                      extent of this alteration is still very obscure. (Reich
                      1951d)

(a radioactive isotope of phosphorus); and later two needles of 1 mg radium each.

www.orgonomie.net

---


Accordingly, his later experiments concentrated on this assumption, and aimed at checking whether very small quantities of radioactive substances, when lying in strong and intensive orgone accumulators in an orgone room,85 would be rendered harmless. The results of this last experiment, called Oranur,86 which was carried out in Reich's laboratory, led him to quite different conclusions, for radioactivity proved harmful at a high concentration of orgone energy (Reich 1951b). Nevertheless, further investigations by Reich in the following years, on the basis of the results of the Oranur experience, proved that in the end orgone energy could convert nuclear energy into a more powerful and fundamentally benign form of energy, called Orur (Reich 1956, p. 14). The results of his first experiments with the Geiger-Müller counter and orgone apparatuses, carried out in the years 1947 and 1948, led him to unexpected findings and to the conclusion that orgone energy could also be used and converted to generate mechanical energy or work.87

During the first experiments, carried out in 1947-1948, Reich established that the Geiger-Müller counter, with which the radioactivity was monitored, initially reacted normally, registering the background count and the gamma radiation of the area. However, the device stopped functioning when it was placed near orgone accumulators. It no longer reacted to background radiation, and not even to small X-ray sources. The apparatus had remained practically incapable of reaction for some weeks, when finally the needle of the pulse counter revolved at a rate of one full rotation per second, corresponding to about 100 pulses per second—a very high value compared to the normal background of 10 to 15 counts per minute. In further measurements Reich obtained a reading of about six to eight thousand counts per minute. Reich recognized that he was possibly witnessing a motor force.88 Somehow, the orgone energy was being converted by the Geiger-Müller counter (Reich 1951b, pp. 36-83) into electromagnetic and mechanical energy. He argued that the motor effect had occurred because the tube of the Geiger-Müller counter, which was constantly exposed to a high orgonotic charge in the laboratory, had absorbed the energy. Since the gas-filled counting tube consisted of an inner cylindrical metal tube and an outer

non-metallic protective layer, usually made of coated glass, the counting tube behaves in essence like a small orgone accumulator.89

Later he replaced the counting tube with a specially constructed vacuum tube (called the Vacor tube). It was equipped with inner parallel aluminum plates, each connected with the cathode and anode respectively. The plates were introduced into the tube (as a rule made of borosilicate glass) at a distance of 4-6 cm from one another. Each plate was 16 cm long and 4 cm wide. The vacuum had a pressure of 0.5 microns, which suffices to exclude the presence of gas. This arrangement functioned like a kind of orgone energy accumulator in a vacuum. By using this improved arrangement, Reich obtained a much more powerful reaction, which shortly thereafter was able to drive a small rotary motor. In another arrangement he used a small orgone accumulator connected to a rotor. He excited the concentrated orgone energy in the interior of the accumulator to drive the rotor with a half-volt current. In fact, Reich found that electrical energy can excite orgone energy:

   In general, a Geiger-Müller tube filled with a mixture of argon, neon, and either
chlorine or bromine gases consists of an anode (positive electrode) located
in the middle of a tubular cathode (negative electrode). The cathode is a
thin-walled metallic cylinder sealed at each end with an insulating disk
to contain the gas. The anode is a wire extending within the cylinder.
A high voltage is applied to the electrodes to create an electric field
inside the chamber. When radiation passes through the chamber and
ionizes the gas, that generates a current pulse. The device processes these pulses
to display the radiation level.

In practice, Reich was able, with all these investigations, to convert an excited concentrated orgone energy field into usable electrical energy, in order to generate work:

"I simply converted orgone into electrical energy."
                      (Reich 1999, p. 401)

However, despite this important discovery, Reich never disclosed the basic theory, the design, and the experimental settings by which he ran the small rotary motor and made mechanical work directly available from the locally available cosmic orgone energy.90 Shortly after the discovery he wrote a very short communication, contained in the book The Cancer Biopathy (Reich 1948, pp. 165f), and a short report a year later, included in the first issue of the Orgone Energy Bulletin (Reich 1949c). The information contained in them was too slight to understand and reproduce the experiments that led to the development of the motor. Nonetheless, in Reich's last book, Contact with Space, there are some indications of how one might develop an orgone motor and perhaps understand the significance of the mysterious and inexplicable Y factor, which according to Reich was essential in the development of the orgone motor. From them one can derive that Reich was able to convert orgone energy into mechanical energy through excited concentrated orgone energy, and that one of the core points of such a conversion was the use of radioactivity. He also stated a minimum radioactive radiation in order to run a motor.


---


In the first chapters of the text of The Oranur Experiment, however, are found many of the tests he carried out by means of the Geiger-Müller counter, and which later led to the development of the first prototypes of the orgone motor. And they can be regarded as introductory work for the development of the final prototype of the orgone motor. That the orgone motor ran was observed by many coworkers and by people who had nothing to do with his researches. The demonstration of the functioning of the orgone motor also took place during the First International Conference, held from August 30 to September 2, 1948, at Orgonon. In his reports Reich described the basic function of the motor only in general lines:

The Western Electric KS-9154 motor used by Reich is a small two-phase rotary motor with a diameter of 2 15/16 inches and a length of 4 1/16 inches. It consists of a laminated steel stator and a rotor (1 inch in diameter and 2.5 inches long), made of a hollow copper cylinder open at one end and connected with a steel shaft at the other end (Reiter 2002).

Reich died without revealing the function Y, an essential component for understanding the functioning of the orgone motor, because he assumed that the average person (the Little Man, as he called him) was not prepared to use so unlimited a source of energy. But what is missing in Reich's reports is not only the meaning of function Y, but also the general theory behind the mode of operation of the motor, of which function Y is only a part.

Among the reports of colleagues who experienced the orgone motor are those of Sharaf, Baker, Wyvell, Neill, and that of his second wife, Ilse Ollendorff. They add only anecdotal information about the orgone motor, and only very few more interesting details are found in addition to those already available.

www.orgonomie.net
                                        202

Generally, it emerges from the reports that the wheel of the motor turned much faster and noiselessly when it was driven only by the orgone energy supplied to it from a Vacor tube or a small orgone accumulator; whereas when connected with a battery, the wheel turned much more slowly, and the rotary movement was accompanied by noises. When powered by orgone energy alone, the rotation of the wheel was influenced by weather conditions. Moreover, Reich explained that he either wanted to commercialize the discovery, or only to carry out investigations and experiments without pursuing marketing. He also affirmed that he did not want to publish the details, including the Y factor. This latter controversial behavior was never considered and discussed by Reich's biographers and students.

After Reich's death, no significant research efforts
were undertaken to rediscover the original orgone motor and its mode of operation,
including the function Y. In the orgonomic literature
only very few papers have appeared in recent years, and they
concentrated mainly on supplying further information and details on
already-known topics—such as particulars of the electric motor used by Reich
(Western Electric KS-9154)—and hypotheses about the
nature of the Y factor (Reiter 2002; see also DeMeo 2002).


---


The only published research that pursued this path further comes from
Correa and Correa (Correa, Correa 2006b; see also Correa, Correa 2006a and

In developing what the authors call an energy conversion system from massfree energy into electrical or kinetic energy, they drew on the work of Tesla, Aspden, De Broglie, and Thom, and only partly on the work of Reich. In the 2007 US patent text they present the philosophy of their invention:

For understanding, it is decisive that the implication of the fact that—apart from local electromagnetic radiation and thermal radiation connected with the movements of molecules (thermo-mechanical energy)—there is at least one further form of energy radiation that is present everywhere, even in space in the absence of matter … And undoubtedly this radiation too is
                     omnipresent and is not subject to relativistic
                     transformations (i.e., Lorentz-invariant). It is not
                     electromagnetic radiation consisting of random
                     phases of transverse waves.

To understand this correctly, … this energy, which is neither
                     electromagnetic nor thermal in itself (and certainly
                     not merely thermo-mechanical), nevertheless has identifiable

In essence, the first subtype, or the first
                     variant, consists of longitudinal, massfree waves that form electrical
                     energy. They could also be called Tesla waves,
                     since it can in fact be shown experimentally that

                     in the form of longitudinal magnetic and electric
                     waves having properties that are not reducible

                     waves," …

One can well characterize the second subtype by naming it
                     massfree heat radiation, since it contributes

                     this contribution occurs independently of the
                     presence of matter, or mass/energy, in space.
                     …


---


Finally, the third subtype can be described as latent massfree
                     energy radiation—since it unfolds neither charge

                     is nevertheless responsible for "true latent heat" or for the "intrinsic
                     potential energy" of a molecule. (Correa,
                     Correa 2007)

www.orgonomie.net
                                          212

Correa and Correa developed a concept of massfree energy free of inertia, similar to Reich's orgone energy, and it can be found everywhere in space. They found that its properties could belong to three massfree energy subtypes or variants, defined in detail as radiant massfree electrical energy, radiant massfree thermal energy, and that responsible for latent heat. However, the authors in their physical model did not mention two other important subtypes of cosmic orgone energy—namely, the gravitational-field variant and, above all, the nuclear-field variant, which seemed extremely important to Reich for the development of a motor force.92

Proceeding from the above assumptions, Correa and Correa, in

while, in reconstructing the archaeology of the Y function, they
came to the conclusion that it could correspond to a thermionic full-wave divider of the Delon,
Greinacher, and Latour circuits, used in the manner of a dual-diode sensor circuit in RF power meters (Correa, Correa
2006b). However, the inventors never provided a public demonstration of the

David Boadella: Orgone energy, love                   93 94

and spaceships (1955)
[Author's preliminary remark]
Since the following lecture was given before a research society for flying saucers, it did not need to justify that flying saucers exist. For the readers of this journal, however, for many of whom the field may be new, a few introductory words are required.


---


The history of science shows that phenomena that do not fit into the orthodox categories of knowledge are frequently rejected, denied, and circumvented, without any attempt being made to consider factual evidence. When Reich discovered orgone, he was told that it was only "statics" or "heat shimmer," that the temperature increase in the accumulator was "only" a question of "convection," and that the accumulator functioned simply through "suggestion." When it was observed that life developed from highly sterilized cultures, he was told that it was really only a question of "Brownian motion" or of "air germs."

This constant explaining-away of objectively verifiable facts prompted Reich to use the catchword "air germs" to characterize every kind of objection to his work in which evidence is ignored and the facts are willfully distorted.

In the field of flying saucers, the "air germs" argument is very widespread and very potent. Accordingly, it is very easy to dismiss flying saucers as "hallucinations," "stratospheric balloons," "meteorites," "light refraction in inversion weather conditions," "spider webs floating in the air," "the planet Venus," "soap bubbles," etc.

While one must concede that a certain proportion of alleged saucer sightings can be explained in this way, there is nevertheless a large number of "Unidentified Flying Objects" that can in no way be "identified" as merely this or that.

93
   Reprint of the translation from the English with the kind permission of the author,
Dr. Boadella. The original essay "Orgone Energy, Love and Space Ships" is found in the

and Jean Ritter in Nottingham, England.
94
   This is the reprint of a lecture read on Saturday, June 18, 1955, at the
invitation of the Flying Saucer Research Society London, in Caxton Hall, Westminster.
The first speech, by Paul Ritter, was a chronological introduction to and
an overview of the work of Wilhelm Reich, whereby the context for this second half
was provided. The chairman was Leslie Shepard. The editors [of Orgonomic
Functionalism]

www.orgonomie.net
                                         214

Albert Einstein held the view that there are only two possibilities:
Either there are no flying saucers—or they come from space.
There is now a sufficient number of credible reports from responsible pilots, meteorologists, astronomers, and radar operators—not to mention many thousands of sightings by laypersons—to remove, in reasonable people, any doubt about the existence of what we call flying saucers.


---


Despite the fact that the usual emotion provoked by the subject of flying saucers is frivolous levity, it is significant that both the Australian and the Canadian governments have sponsored research on flying saucers; and that a group from the natural-science faculty of the University of Ohio, after a strictly objective investigation of the subject, came to the conclusion that the objects are material and under intelligent control.

It is regrettable, but telling, that the connection with space attracts many people in large numbers whose interest lies only in the sensational and the unorthodox. In this field there have been pranksters and dangerous charlatans who have made sweeping and unfounded claims, but there have been such also in medicine and in every other area of science. The distortion, one should remember, is grounded in the human structure, not in science.

www.orgonomie.net
                                         215

During the last hour you have heard a description of the method of orgonomic functionalism and of some of the achievements it has made possible. This has created the right context for following the relationship that I wish to establish this evening: the relationship between orgone energy and many of the phenomena that characterize reports of flying saucers and sightings.

First of all, however, I wish to make clear that orgone research has not yet reached any definitive conclusions about flying saucers, so that anyone who has come here this evening in the hope of definitive conclusions will be disappointed. What the findings about orgone in other areas do in fact make possible, however, is a new access to the whole problem of Unidentified Flying Objects and the aura of mystery that surrounds them. This approach is based concretely on the factual discoveries that have been made by orgone research so far. I believe that this approach, although it is still in the phase of forming assumptions and testing hypotheses, will in the long run be most fruitful in solving many of the problems provoked by the presence of flying saucers.


---


Among all the thousands of reports in the files of the roughly twenty organizations that since about 1947 have taken up the study of flying saucers, certain features occur with great frequency. Today I would like to single out four of them for particular consideration: First, the existence of a kind of radiation; second, the presence of a kind of radiation effect; third, the fact of the great speeds very often possible in complete silence; and fourth, the manner of movement connected with the saucers. Reich first drew attention to these properties and their relation to orgonotic functions in CORE, Vol. 6, published in July 1954 [Reich 1954b].

www.orgonomie.net
                                         216

PART 1: RADIATION
The British Flying Saucer Bureau has carried out a statistical analysis of all the sightings contained in its files. From this analysis it emerges that 78% of all night sightings describe the observed objects as luminous. The luminosity apparently ranges from a dull glow to a brilliant, blazing radiance. The color seems to range from bluish-gray or bluish-green through violet to red, orange, and white. This great variety of radiation is one of the most confusing properties of the saucers. Moreover, a number of saucers have been observed to change color while being watched. Here it is interesting that this change of color always seems to be connected with a change of movement.

One sighting, which illuminates this relation and at the same time many other features of the flying saucers that I shall deal with, is worth quoting in full, since it is one of the least known and yet one of the most vivid reports I have so far seen.

A construction engineer from the small mining town of Kerkrade in Holland sent the following report to the journal Civilian Saucer Investigation (Vol. 4):


---


I went for a walk around midnight. Everything was quiet. Suddenly
                     my attention was drawn to a blue-green light of medium
                     intensity that ran across the sky in the east. It came
                     down in an elegant dive and then slowed down.
                     At first I thought it was a meteorite, but it left
                     no fiery trail, as they do. It came slowly toward me,
                     and so near that I could see it very distinctly. I
                     have made a test: if you want to know how large I
                     saw it, take a plate, hold it at arm's length,
                     then raise the plate somewhat higher than the crown of your skull and
                     look at it. That is exactly the relative size of the object
                     that I saw. During the flight, and when it
                     stood still, no sound was to be heard. Its color was gray.
                     As it came down, it gave off a blue-green light. As soon as it
                     had stopped, the light disappeared. After I had
                     looked at it intensely for a
                     few minutes, I felt a very
                     unpleasant feeling in my eyes; they felt irritated—the same feeling as
                     after one has stared for a long time at a
                     bad television screen. Suddenly it shot
                     off, executing several sharp angles. At the same
                     time the blue-green hue appeared again. All in all it was
                     visible for a little more than a minute.

I would now like to go into the properties of orgone radiation.

When Reich was working on his bion experiments in 1937, he developed a violent
conjunctivitis in the eye with which he had observed his culture preparations through
the microscope. The same cultures also produced a reddening of the skin. In order to isolate

www.orgonomie.net
                                       217

them and to learn more about the radiation that emanated from them, he took
the cultures and brought them into a dark cellar room, in order to make observations
there.

And so he described what he saw:


---


The observations in the dark were somehow "eerie."
                    The room became, after the eyes had grown accustomed, gray-bluish instead of absolutely
                    black. I saw misty veils, bluish
                    streaks of light, and flying points. Deep violet light impressions
                    seemed to come, as it were, out of the walls and from objects.
                    These light impressions, throughout blue or
                    blue-green, became stronger; the individual streaks and dots
                    became larger when I held a magnifying glass before my eyes.
                    Dark glasses weakened the impressions. (…) After one
                    or two hours' stay in the cellar room, my
                    eyes ached and became reddened. One evening I spent five
                    hours in the cellar room in one stretch. After about two hours
                    I could distinctly see my palm glowing, likewise
                    my shirt sleeves and (in the mirror) also the hair of my head. The
                    blue shimmer lay, like an indistinct, slowly moving,
                    gray-blue luminous haze, around my figure and around
                    objects in the room. I confess that I was frightened. (…) The
                    radiation irritated the optic nerves very much. A businessman who had
                    procured an apparatus for me and once took part, said: "It
                    is to me as if I had looked at the sun for a long time." (Reich 1948,
                    pp. 104f)

The strength of this effect was the immediate stimulus for the construction of the first
experimental orgone accumulator, and the starting point for the many techniques
for objectively measuring, recording, and
demonstrating the existence of the energy. Many experiments in the orgone accumulator led to the assumption
that orgone energy exists in three basic states: bluish-gray, mistlike
formations; deep blue-violet points of light; and fast whitish rays. It
changes from the mistlike to the raylike state when it is strongly excited or irritated by a
stimulus. The main stimuli are metallic
substances, the proximity of another orgone system (for example, the presence
of a human being in the accumulator), or an electrical discharge. To these was
added later the most intensive stimulus of all—nuclear energy.

The clearest proof both of the fundamental blue color of orgone, and of the
shift to white when irritated, is provided by some experiments
that Reich carried out with a standard vacuum tube, such as is used in electronic
research. He found that vacuum tubes that could soak up orgone in an accumulator over several
weeks glow bluish-violet
when a voltage of 600-700 volts is passed through them. Moreover, the
radiation is brighter at the electrodes, up to white, and tends more toward
bluish violet toward the middle of the tube. If the voltage—that is, the
excitation stimulus—is raised, the whole tube finally glows white.


---


www.orgonomie.net
                                         218

During the Oranur experiment, in which the orgone was irritated by small quantities of radium and radioactive phosphorus, a further form was discovered that orgone assumes under extreme irritation. Among the many disturbing effects of the Oranur experiment was the visual impression of red
patches of light in the presence of strongly irritated orgone. This was so unmistakable and
occurred so regularly that the reddish radiation was regarded as a warning sign throughout the
entire experiment, and was one of the signals for the immediate
evacuation of all buildings in which the accumulation of orgone was very strong.

The existence of this kind of reaction can help to explain the many reports of
irritations of the optic nerve found in the files on flying saucers.
The following is typical.

The journal Civilian Saucer Investigation (published 1954) reports that in
the year 1949 a college graduate who saw a saucer testified: "The light
that comes from the windows I would describe as a bluish white, similar
to the color of a welding light. After the thing had disappeared, I became
aware that it shimmered reddishly before my eyes, as if I had stared at a
light bulb. If the light had been like welding, I believe
that the time I looked at it would have ruined my eyes."

What conclusions, if any, can be drawn from these very relevant
"light" properties of orgone? Reich was very impressed by
the similarity between what he observed in his vacuum tube and the
process of twilight. It is known that the earth has an orgone envelope, and the
assumption seemed conclusive that it is not light itself that comes from the
sun, but a non-luminous excitation that "sets" the orgone envelope
into radiation. Light itself would then be understood as a local function of the radiation
and excitation of orgone. Differences in the radiation
would then reflect differences in the intensity of the excitation—different
degrees of orgonotic charge.

It is only consistent that the radiation of flying saucers should be connected with their
movement, and that different forms of movement
should involve different states of charge, both in the object itself and in the orgone of the
surrounding atmosphere.

I would like to close my explanations of radiation with a
description of the light that emanates from a flying saucer. It points
very strongly to the radiation having that soft, living quality that we
associate with daylight, and that Reich found in his vacuum tubes.


---


A woman in Tucson, Arizona (August 1950) had the following to say: "The light
was bright, but not with the hard brilliance of an ordinary electric light. It
seemed to come from the top and the sides in a softer, glowing light.
As it moved away from us and floated over the mountain, it looked
like a gently glowing ball" (from: Civilian Saucer Investigation, Vol. 4).

www.orgonomie.net
                                         219

PART 2: RADIATION EFFECTS
I now come to a feature that is very closely related to the radiation, and yet is clearly to be distinguished from it in its effects.
Light effects are only one of the properties of flying saucers that point to
radiation, but there are many others.

Listen now to this description of the flying saucer from someone who was more impressed by its vibrational effect than by the light phenomena.


---


In the south, in the form of an equilateral triangle, I noticed
                     three illuminated bodies, of which I knew
                     that they were not
                     stars. For a moment I took them for aircraft,
                     but then they moved too fast even for jet planes.
                     Suddenly one of them (the one landing) detached
                     from the formation and seemed to come straight toward me. I was,
                     to be honest, appalled, and threw myself on the ground and
                     pondered what a bad spot this was, should
                     it crash. Suddenly I had the feeling that I had nothing to
                     fear, and I rolled over. There it was, a little
                     to the left (east) of me, apparently stationary. It was a few
                     hundred feet from the ground. I would also estimate
                     that it was between 300 and 400 feet in diameter. It seemed
                     to have windows, which looked roughly twice as long as wide.
                     I noticed that the other two had drawn northward,
                     but had become stationary. The near one did not
                     rotate. The longer I looked at it, however, the more I
                     had a feeling of vibration. I would estimate the thickness
                     at a minimum of 75 feet and a maximum of 100 feet.
                     It was absolutely noiseless, but I had the deep-seated
                     impression that it embodied an enormous energy. The light
                     that came from the windows I would describe as blue-white;
                     the color of a welding light. Thereafter (following
                     my feeling of vibration) it was such that I (very
                     hard to describe) noticed a very, very forceful
                     vibrational force. Everything, myself included, seemed
                     to react to this vibrational force. I can dimly
                     recall the most extraordinary thoughts that this
                     vibrational force seemed to produce (that is the best way
                     I can describe it). One such thought was that with the help of this
                     force, gravity meant nothing. I don't know how long
                     I lay there before the thing sped away northward with incredible
                     speed, and, just as inexplicably, the vibrational feelings seemed
                     to subside and disappear. It took up its leading position in the triangle, and
                     all lassitude vanished. All three rushed off at the same
                     breakneck speed. (from: Civilian Saucer
                     Investigation, Vol. 4)

www.orgonomie.net
                                          220


---


What stands out most from this report is the difficulty of putting into words the effect of the oscillations and vibrations that one felt, and the reaction that the man himself experienced. The report reads as if his own energy had undergone a literally "exciting" event. One cannot help but sense that two energy systems were involved—that of the saucer and that of the observer. This stands in full accord with what is known of orgone energy.

A similar indication that a strong radiation effect is at work is
found in the report (mentioned by Leonard Cramp in his book Space,
Gravity and the Flying Saucer [Cramp 1954]) of a 28-year-old pharmacist, who was
traveling by car into the town of Lufkin, Texas, when he noticed an object hovering more than
two hundred feet above him. It whirled around like a flying
saucer, described an angle of fifty degrees, and raced off. Five minutes
later his face began to burn. This is by no means an isolated case, for
Graham Knewstub of the British Flying Saucer Bureau has reported that "there
are well-documented cases of observers who suffered injuries
(mainly burns) from coming too close to flying saucers."

I would now like to compare the oscillating, vibrating, and "burning" feelings described in the two reports I have just presented to you with the experience of people who have sat in the orgone accumulator. Naturally we would expect these to be much milder in their expression, but basically of the same kind. I have an accumulator at home, and was able to confirm for myself what Reich found. I have asked a number of people who knew nothing about orgone to sit in it and tell me what they felt. Here are some typical answers:

1. A thirteen-year-old girl: "Gee, it's like needles. It's as if you had a cold and you're all hot." She said that when she brought her fingers nearer to the wall, it made "my fingers throb a little." And a very interesting remark: "It's like a kind of humming." Humming was, it seemed, the only way she could make the feeling of vibration comprehensible to me.

2. A thirty-year-old woman reacted as follows: "It's like when you're asleep—a kind of getting up. My neck is hot, my arms are going pink." That was quite distinct when she came out somewhat later. "I know—it's like when you have the flu or something, and you don't know whether you're hot or cold. The nearer you come to the walls, the more you get this feeling. God, it could burn you."

3. Other comments from various people include: "I can feel my muscles relaxing, the way they do in front of the fire"; "You feel that if you touched the wall, it would sizzle"; and: "I feel as if I had been lying in the sun—my face and my neck are burning."


---


When we remember that the walls of the orgone accumulator are very cold, these comments become all the more remarkable. A vibrating force that tingles and prickles, that evokes a feeling like hot flashes or sun exposure and a clearly visible reddening of the skin, that is moreover connected with impressions of blue to violet points of light and streaks of light—all this is felt by

www.orgonomie.net
                                         221

people who know nothing of orgone when they sit in an orgone accumulator.

There is also a case in the publications of the Orgone Institute, of a
woman who sat in the orgone accumulator and smeared an ointment on her face. This
seemed to attract the orgone very powerfully, for her face became considerably inflamed
and remained sore for some days afterward.

Furthermore, as a consequence of many observations with his bions, in the
atmosphere, and with fluorescent gas tubes, Reich had come in 1944 to the conclusion
that pulsation is a fundamental property of orgone. It was established
that every orgonotic system involves an orgone field that expands
and contracts with a certain rhythm. We shall now
see what evidence there is that saucers have an energetic field.

There are frequent reports of magnetic and electrical devices not
functioning in the vicinity of flying saucers. Indeed, one may speculate
how many unexplained aircraft crashes could be attributable to an unknown FIELD effect from
unseen saucers. One of these crashes seems to
point to this kind of thing. I mean, of course, Captain Mantell's crash
over Fort Knox, at the beginning of 1948, after he had chased a huge, shiny, metallic
cigar-shaped object.

Observations of the behavior of orgone in this respect exist at the moment only
in very small numbers. But Reich reports that in the early days, when he was trying to
isolate the orgone in his cellar, all metal objects—such as scissors, pliers,
needles, etc.—became highly magnetic. More can be learned from the Oranur
experiment.


---


This experiment, in which orgone was irritated with nuclear material, produced a
force field that was much larger than the quantity of radium used in the
experiment could explain. Only one milligram of radium was used, and
this was shielded by a 1/2-inch lead container. Nevertheless, the
walls of the metal-lined room in which the experiment was carried out
very soon became unbearably charged, and radiated at a distance of 16
feet from the lead container. The air was heavy and oppressive, and the coworkers suffered
from headaches and nausea. But this effect ceased as soon as the radium
was taken out of the accumulator and brought into a normal room.
Obviously, the radium alone, still in its shielding, had not produced this
intensive radiation effect. Remarkable was that even after the
removal of the radium, the reaction persisted and even rapidly expanded. The
greatly widened orgone field could be demonstrated in many ways: the
radiation of fluorescent gas could normally be produced by bringing one's hand near
the glass tube. Now this reaction could be produced at a distance of two feet from the tube, instead of the normal two inches. The Geiger counter measured a strong rise in the background radiation in the
atmosphere of the orgone room, long after the radium had been removed from the orgone room,
while the room in which the radium was located gave a much
weaker reaction. This highly charged, radiant orgone field extended

www.orgonomie.net
                                         222

over a distance of 50 to 100 feet beyond the outer walls of the laboratory, and was connected with an unusual and inexplicable decay in the
granite walls of the laboratory.

Reich describes these walls as follows:

In one and the same rock, which only a few months before had been
                     smooth and gray, one sees black, round or striped
                     spots, which spread either from the edge toward the center or vice versa.
                     With many stones, the surfaces have lost their
                     smoothness; the rock surface looks spongy,
                     as if holes had been drilled into the rock, and countless
                     holes of a diameter and depth of roughly
                     1/16 or 1/8 inch had been worked into the surface.
                     [Reich 1953d]

Let us now hear what Leonard Cramp has to say about the Mantell crash:


---


The wreckage of Captain Mantell's machine came down over a wide
                     area, and, according to rumors, quite inexplicable cuts
                     were discovered during the examination of the parts—very
                     tiny, and accompanied by very small holes.
                     This was not merely a case of ordinary failure of the
                     structure; it was possibly failure at the molecular
                     level, due to a breakdown of natural
                     cohesion, caused by penetration into the border region

                     (Cramp 1954, p. 94)

I will conclude what I have to say about the radiation effects of saucers and of orgone with a few words about the effect of radiation on photographic
plates. The March 1955 issue of the British journal Flying Saucer News
contains a most interesting article entitled "Fuzzy
Photos – radiation?", which refers to three photographs of saucers from Delft, Helsinki,
and New Zealand. Each of these photos shows a very
distinct blurred ring, or halo, surrounding the saucer.

This corresponds very exactly to the fact that Reich found similar mist- or
cloudlike shadows when, for example, he exposed a photographic plate
to the influence of bion cultures or the orgone field between the palms. The excited orgone field seems to have the property of hindering the
passage of normal light rays, and this could well be the
root of many puzzling effects on saucer photographs.

www.orgonomie.net
                                         223

PART 3: SPEED AND
NOISELESSNESS
I shall now discuss the third property of the saucers that I am selecting this evening—their speed of movement, and the fact that this
movement is normally noiseless. Harold Wilkins gives a useful example
of this feature:


---


In April 1949 a team of scientists and
                    naval officers tracked the flight of a weather balloon northwest of the
                    White Sands proving ground. The ascent was monitored with
                    telescope, theodolite, and stopwatch. The balloon was
                    high up when the man with the telescope was startled. He
                    turned his telescope eastward and observed a single
                    disc approaching the upward path of the balloon.
                    While he did this, a scientist tracked the disc
                    through a theodolite. The strange object appeared to be an
                    ellipsoid, roughly 100 feet wide and roughly 56
                    miles high. Now a ballistics expert calculated the
                    speed of the disc. It was five miles per
                    second. No engine has yet been invented on earth
                    that will make a machine move at this
                    fantastic speed. That is a
                    speed of 18,000 miles per hour. Then came a
                    further astonishing incident. The thing suddenly shot
                    upward, and in ten seconds had increased its height above the earth
                    by 25 miles. Again, mathematicians,
                    astrophysicists, and ballistics experts calculated that the mighty force
                    exerted in this tremendous acceleration corresponded to
                    twenty times the attraction of the earth's gravitational
                    field. No human being of our earth could endure
                    such gravity without every cell in the body being
                    torn apart. The strange disc was in sight for approximately one
                    minute. (Wilkins 1954)

It is interesting that one of the most famous pioneers in the field of
rocket research, Professor Hermann Oberth, is said to have stated that the behavior
of flying saucers rules out every kind of propulsion known to us,
including any kind of rocket drive.

Does orgone research offer any facts that can throw light on the colossal
speeds attained by the saucers?

Can orgone energy drive a motor? The answer is yes.

www.orgonomie.net
                                           224


---


In the year 1947, Reich acquired a Geiger-Müller counter, in order to see what effect, if any, orgone would have on it.95 For many weeks the counter did not react. It seemed that it had to soak up orgone for a considerable time before it began to react. Then, in August, the first strong reactions occurred. When the battery was switched on, the counter recorded 100 pulses
per second, or one rotation per second. By comparison, the
normal count of background radiation is 25-30 pulses per minute, or one pulse
every 2 seconds; and the maximum count that had been attained at that time from nuclear sources
lay at 50 pulses per second, or exactly half of what
Reich achieved without the use of any nuclear source at all. He
found that the pulses ceased as soon as he took the Geiger counter out of the accumulator, and began again as soon as he put it back in.

Reich now bought a so-called "scaler unit," which made it possible to count only every 32nd
pulse, and thus to permit the recording of a far larger range of activity than with the normal Geiger counter. At the same time he wanted to find
out whether orgone in a vacuum would produce a higher or a lower
rotation speed on his counter. The maximum number of
pulses he had obtained with the gas-filled counter was 3,200 per
minute. With the vacuum tube, the pulse rate accelerated enormously, and required
a far lower initial voltage to trigger the effect. I must point
out that, from the standpoint of nuclear physics, this is an unbelievable achievement, since the Geiger counter has never before triggered a reaction when there is no gas
in the tube, and therefore no gas particles can be ionized. The
details of the entire course of the experiment, which led into the development of the
Geiger counter and the vacuum tubes, would prove too technical and very
tedious, so I will shorten the story of the orgonotic motor force by saying that by 1951 a maximum count rate of the vacuum tubes reached the dizzying figure of 25,000 pulses per second under ideal
atmospheric conditions. The enormity of this
reaction should not make us incredulous. Since orgone is the cosmic energy, since
it penetrates the whole of space, and since there is such a thing as an orgone envelope around the
earth, it should logically follow that orgone energy in space is also the force
that governs the rotation and revolution of the planets.

Such a force could well be relevant to the high speeds attained by flying saucers, and also points to why the saucers are so often described as noiseless. The only sound that is heard
when orgone energy is at work is the intended sound of the mechanical
recording device. Orgone itself is noiseless.

95
  A Geiger counter is the common instrument for measuring the intensity of radioactive
deposits.
