PART 4: FORM OF MOVEMENT
I now turn to the fourth distinguishing feature of the saucers, one of the most difficult to understand—their form of movement. Toward the end of his book on space, gravity, and flying saucers (Space, Gravity and the Flying Saucer), Leonard Cramp makes the following remarkable statement:

We have now reached a phase in which we can bring before our minds
                     what is undoubtedly the most important
                     key to the phenomena connected with the flying
                     saucer. We all know that in the core of
                     matter itself the electron revolves in an orbit. We
                     have seen that dust particles can be made to rotate through the influence of light.
                     Satellites, planets,
                     suns, and even galaxies rotate, and with this
                     universal phenomenon the so-called force of gravity
                     is connected. And—flying saucers rotate too.
                     (Cramp 1954, p. 165)

They not only rotate, but in their movement from place to place they have frequently
been described as "pendulum-like" or "zigzag-shaped" or like "a stone skipping
over a mill pond." Cramp suggests that this is simply the visual
effect of an object moving on an elongated spiral.

If we assume that flying saucers use orgone energy, then
we should expect to find a relation between orgone energy and spirality.
Do we in fact find such a relation?

At the beginning of 1940, Reich had recorded his impression that the pulsating points
of violet light that he had observed in the orgone darkroom seemed to revolve in the form
of a certain trajectory, as "spiral waves." Many years
later he was able to solve certain problems presented by natural phenomena such as the
galaxy and the aurora by assuming that two
orgone currents, initially with different directions, flow toward each other,
converge, and finally superimpose. The form that the two
superimposing currents assume is that of a spiral.

This is confirmed by Reich's study of the origin of hurricanes. Reich
was able to show that a hurricane likewise arises through the superimposition of two
orgone currents, whereby the eye of the hurricane corresponds to the center of the
resulting spiral. This research consistently developed into a practical technique for changing the weather.

We observe with interest that Reich's conjectures here are largely confirmed by the researches of a man named James Pettigrew [1834-1908—P.N.], an English physician who was able to show in great detail how an astonishing
variety of natural manifestations are in fact spiral-shaped. Independently of
Reich, he drew attention to the similarity in basic form between phenomena such as
the spiral nebulae, plant growth, a tornado, and the development and


---


www.orgonomie.net
                                         226

structure of the various bones, muscles, and tissues of the human
body.

His investigations were so thorough and comprehensive that one may with good right regard spirality as a fundamental feature of nature. The question
then reads: What, in the human body and in the galaxy, in the aurora and in the
flying saucer, moves in spiral form?

Paul Ritter has accomplished something with regard to the examination of the evidence of Pettigrew
and many other researchers who have written about spiral formations,
and has shown how their work makes a more comprehensive sense against the background of orgonotic functions.

The exact mechanics of the movement of the flying saucer must still be
investigated. At this point it suffices to establish the fact that its
oscillating, spiral-shaped movement stands in full accord with what
was discovered regarding the movement of orgone in other functional areas.

This brings me to the end of the first section of my lecture, and rounds off what I have to say about the observed properties of saucers and their relation
to orgone.

It has turned out that the main features of the flying saucers correspond, in an astonishing way, to the functions of orgone energy on which Reich had worked for many
years before there was any such thing as a "saucer problem"
at all. Moreover, the relations that Reich first discovered in 1953, and that I have worked out this evening, developed unexpectedly out of this
earlier work, without any particular intention, or any attempt, to solve a
"saucer problem."

In the remaining time I would like to go briefly into the relation of orgone to
gravity, and into the work of Leonard Cramp. What proof is there
that orgone in the atmosphere can be technologically controlled and steered? And what moved the flying saucers to visit our atmosphere since
about 1947 in such numbers?

www.orgonomie.net
                                         227

PART 5
It is not easy to speak about the relation of gravitation to orgone, because
no one has yet fully answered the question: What is gravity? Newton's
theory is no longer accepted as final, and in recent years there have been
many new attempts to arrive at a more fundamental understanding of what
gravity is. Among these we must count Einstein's unified field theory,
Cramp's hypothesis, and the orgonomic theory of Reich.


---


Reich's theory of gravitation is in itself fairly easy to understand, but it has not yet been translated into practically utilizable terms. It is also very difficult to explain three-dimensional concepts satisfactorily without the help of diagrams or models. He criticizes the notion that dead, inert mass can exert a gravitational force, and suggests that gravitation can rather be understood as a function of two superimposing orgone currents, which he was able to demonstrate through the investigation of hurricanes. According to this theory, neither does the moon circle the earth, nor the earth the sun, since both the sun
and the earth move themselves through space. The closed orbit occurs
only when one makes the center of the circle or of the ellipse stationary. Anyone
who takes an orange and a pea and likes to experiment can pursue this for himself. The form of the paths of earth and sun is that of two lines that
converge, diverge, and come together again and again. Reich's
theory assumes that the path of the earth, as well as that of the sun, is determined in each case by its own orgone current, and that it is therefore these two
currents that find each other, superimpose, and separate again.

Cramp's theory of gravitation is in many respects more ingenious and more worked-out than Reich's, but one cannot help but feel that Cramp wrestles with an enormous handicap. His theory, which relates to the "space aether," remains—however valuable and stimulating it may be—as a concept an
abstraction. In place of a purely theoretical and insubstantial aether, we have
a practically utilizable and demonstrable orgone energy.

Although the hypothesis of gravitation as a function of two superimposing
orgone currents may appear rather speculative, its value lies in the fact that it opens up, for anyone who has sufficiently understood the behavior of orgone in space, the possibility of controlling gravitation, or of counteracting it.

Can atmospheric orgone be utilized at all in a directed manner?
Once again, the answer is yes.

Through the use of long steel tubes, 30-40 feet long, Reich succeeded
in altering the concentration of orgone in the sky above us. Out of
this basic technique an entirely new branch of work has developed—namely,
weather control.

This work consists of a number of ingenious operations that work according
to the principle of dispersing cloud formations by drawing energy out of them, or of drawing energy from a clear part of the sky in such a way that an

www.orgonomie.net
                                         228

energy flow is produced from a lower to a higher potential, in order thereby to induce the formation of clouds and precipitation in drought-stricken areas.


---


Since the middle of the year 1953, the steel tubes have been used more or less continuously
to relieve the drought-stricken areas on the northeast coast of the USA. Among the most spectacular results achieved so far in this area
belongs a hurricane that last October threatened to strike
New York, and that was successfully diverted out to the open sea. The New York weather bureau
announced that it would be a miracle if the hurricane did not hit the city head-on,
since it had shown no signs of swerving out to the open sea.
The next morning Boston radio commented: "The nearly impossible has
happened—the storm is turning away … Last night it was said that only a
miracle could prevent the hurricane from striking New York. New York has gotten
its miracle. It looks as if the same could befall New England." Five hours later they reported: "The hurricane has moved on eastward … no damages … slight
damages … for all of New England the
danger is fully averted, except for the coast of Maine … The
devastations that only a few hours earlier seemed to be
immediately before us have been spared us."

When one reads the report in the newest bulletin of the Orgone Institute (Core Vol. 7, Nos.
1, 2 [Reich 1955c]), one sees how this hurricane was approached, the
details of the technique for diverting it, and one suddenly becomes aware
of the fact that for the first time orgone research is issuing into cosmic functions,
functions beyond the globe of the earth. It is interesting to compare the mighty forces that the "cloudbuster" taps with what the Atomic Energy Commission
of the United States has to say about the usefulness of atomic power as a means of controlling
hurricanes.

In their report on the effects of atomic weapons, they write:

It has been proposed that certain
                     destructive natural phenomena such as hurricanes, tornadoes, or
                     cold waves could be averted through a sudden release of the great
                     quantities of energy delivered by an atomic bomb,
                     but it is very doubtful whether this could be done,
                     because the quantities of energy that appear
                     necessary are of a higher order of magnitude than those
                     presently attainable. (from The Effects of Atomic
                     Weapons)

Reich is unambiguous in his reports that handling the
enormous force that resides in orgone requires an attitude of the utmost humility, and
burdens the operators with responsibility.

In his first technical report on weather control, he gives his coworkers
the following advice:

Never try to impress anyone with cloudbusting.
                     Rainmaking and cloudbusting are not done for fun. The

www.orgonomie.net
                                         229


---


orgone envelope with which you are dealing when you draw
                     energy from the atmosphere is an energetic continuum
                     of high potency. You could unintentionally do
                     damage. Never do anything that is not absolutely necessary.
                     In cloudbusting, no rain is "produced" and no
                     clouds are "destroyed"—in short, one does not play God. One merely
                     helps nature somewhat in its natural course. (Reich
                     1954b, p. 155)

In his book on flying saucers, Cramp explains: "Even in the aether itself
there is a power that lies beyond our wildest dreams. Nor is it
a matter of getting something for nothing, as some may think. If we consider
the physical universe, just as we have it before us, as a vast expanse of
energy—we who move about in this universe can add nothing
to it, nor destroy anything. At most, we can change the form in which it
confronts us" (Cramp 1954, p. 99). The orgone weather experiments prove the
truth of this statement and represent the first step toward tapping this
mighty reservoir of force. Those who build and use spaceships, from
whatever region of space they may come, have gone one step
further in this direction.

We can now pose our final question for this evening. Why do they now come so frequently to our little planet—what draws them here?

A number of authors have proposed that the development of our own
atomic capabilities would certainly give some near neighbors in space cause
for concern. Shortly before his death, Professor Einstein made it clear that
man now held sufficient power in his hands to bring the total annihilation
of life on our planet within the range of the technically feasible.

Gerald Heard went a step further and proposed that the energy released
by a hydrogen bomb exploding under water could generate so much instability
that, as a result, the matter of the earth itself could disintegrate. The
occupants of the spaceships would then be just as interested in our future as
we should be if, for example, the planet Mars were soon to explode, with all
the effects this would have on our own position in the solar system.

However valid Heard's ideas may be, orgone research offers us a somewhat differently oriented approach. During the
Oranur experiment, very strong atmospheric and biological effects
were observed. The nuclear material used seemed to excite the orgone in the atmosphere to such an extent that it became detrimental to health and
life-threatening. Reich called this the Deadly Orgone Reaction.


---


In this state, the orgone charge of the atmosphere is strongly increased, with
at the same time a strongly reduced energy pulsation. A stagnation and rigidity took the place of the accustomed, healthy, living state of the atmosphere. Reich
described his impression of the state of emergency that arose in the vicinity of his laboratory
as follows:

www.orgonomie.net
                                         230

Stillness and desolation spread over the landscape, the
                     affected area being fairly sharply delimited from the unaffected
                     surroundings. The stillness expresses itself
                     in the fact that all life in the atmosphere seems to
                     die away. The birds stop singing, the frogs stop
                     croaking. The birds fly low or hide in the
                     trees. The animals crawl along the ground much more slowly than usual,
                     and the leaves and the evergreen needles of the trees
                     look very "sad"; they droop and lose their
                     inner tension and the ability to straighten up again.
                     The trees look black, as if they were dying. One has,
                     in fact, the impression of blackness, or rather of desolation.
                     … One cannot shake off the feeling that this is death—the "desolate
                     death," as some express it. (Reich 1952a, pp. 450f)

This deadly orgone reaction, first discovered
during the Oranur experiment, occurs whenever the orgone is irritated by nuclear material. Thus every atomic explosion produced by us has, besides all its known
effects, also led to previously UNKNOWN effects on the orgone metabolism
of our atmosphere. All of them have contributed to triggering the deadly
orgone reaction. The effect is cumulative, often diffuse, and hard to connect
with specific explosions.

The much-discussed relation of atomic explosions to the noticeable deterioration
of the weather in the world becomes understandable, however, in view of what we know about orgone and
weather.


---


From the viewpoint of orgone metabolism and the inhibited, stagnating deadly
reaction, a whole series of problems becomes understandable. Why the
drought problem in the USA has reached the proportions of a great national emergency,
with four-fifths of the country susceptible to drought, in contrast to one
fifth only ten years ago. For us in England it may be difficult to become aware of
the severity of the desert problem in the world, or to recognize that
it is connected with a reduction of orgone pulsation. It is true, there have
always been deserts here and there somewhere. The decisive point for us in 1955 is
that atomic radiation has the power greatly to multiply what Reich has called the "desolate death"
in the atmosphere. We are experiencing this desolate death in another
form: in the increasing loss of pulsation in our seasons, in the
tendency toward dangerous accumulations of smog, even in rural districts where
there is no smoke from factories for many kilometers around.
Smogged clouds and turbid drizzle, the absence of any genuine
summer—these too point to a standstill of the healthy flow of energy.
Only last week (on July 11) the Warwickshire section of the National
Farmers' Union called for a global investigation of the effects of atomic explosions
on the weather by scientists. In support of their claim that
something abnormal had thrown the weather out of balance, they pointed
to the following four facts:

1. For four years there has been no good season.

www.orgonomie.net
                                         231

2. Hundreds of acres of land have become unusable through too much rain.
3. Sheep and cattle are infertile in greater numbers than ever before.
4. The lambing season is the worst in living memory.

In the conclusion to their report, they declare that if the weather did not improve in the next two years, agriculture as we know it today would disappear.

This seems far removed from spaceships. But if orgone reacts so
sensitively to nuclear material, there is every reason why the
present developments on earth should be of more than
ordinary interest to the spaceships. If one milligram of radium in a 20-layer orgone accumulator can produce an atmospheric reaction measurable as an
increase in background radiation within a radius of 300 miles of Reich's
laboratory, we can only guess what effects a
hydrogen bomb explosion would have on the orgone metabolism of space.

The deadly orgone reaction is not limited to weather and vegetation.
It also produces a peculiar emotional strain in animals and
humans.


---


There is a strange incident that Wilkins mentioned in his book on flying
saucers, which seems to relate directly to the deadly orgone sickness. In January 1954, 200 sheep in the vicinity of Gravesend were thrown into
obvious panic, and were found the next morning drowned in
drainage ditches on the Isle of Grain. Wilkins cites other cases where
sheep suddenly fell into panic, in one case over an area of 220
square miles. Wilkins comments on these incidents with these words:

While there is no proof that flying
                     saucers were the cause of this panic, it must be
                     noted that birds—for example, pigeons—and sheep are very
                     sensitive to sky phenomena. [Wilkins
                     1954]

What he has to say about pigeons is still more relevant. In November 1953, only 3 of 350 carrier pigeons returned to their lofts, after being released from
Bookaloo in South Australia. It turned out that the sixth sense that pigeons and other birds normally use to find their way over
great distances had somehow vanished or been impaired by the recently occurring explosion
of an atomic bomb in the region of Woomera, likewise in South
Australia. This would suggest that the directional instinct of birds is a kind of ORGONOTIC SENSE, and
that the sense of orientation is connected with the normal flow of the orgone energy envelope.
Has this envelope developed, so to speak, a local sickness in the region of the
atomic explosions? That would correspond to placing a strong magnet under the
compass of a seafarer. One can hardly escape the question of to what extent the deadly reaction of orgone to nuclear irritation might itself signify a real or
potential disturbance of the navigation of flying saucers through space.

www.orgonomie.net
                                            232

If birds have a natural orgonotic sense of direction, and spaceships
use the orgone currents of the universe as their main traffic routes, one would expect to find in birds a certain sensitivity to the presence of
flying saucers, and Wilkins gives in his book one or two
cases of birds that sensed the presence of saucers of which the
people on the ground were unaware.

The effect of deadly orgone on humans was investigated in depth
by Reich. The Oranur experiment produced in acute form what the "bleakness"
in the atmosphere produces in chronic form. Leslie Shepard has collected some
interesting observations on the reactions of sensitive people to
weather swings, and he has shown how this is connected with the orgone flow
(cf. this journal, Vol. II, No. 4).96 Reduced pulsation in
the atmosphere leads to reduced pulsation in humans. The reactions
to it vary with the individuals. Reich presents examples such as the following:


---


Many people do not know, or cannot explain to themselves,
                        what is the matter with them. They speak of "heat" or of
                        "some atomic dust" or simply of "bad
                        air." … "Something is not right in the air," one hears
                        them say, or "something is going on"—a statement
                        from which both a perception and a suspicion speak.
                        "I can't get any air," or "it hits me like a stone
                        when I enter my shop in the morning," etc. Sometimes the
                        same question must be asked several times before one obtains the
                        hesitant, confused answer: "Well, if it interests you—I sometimes have the feeling as if something were enclosing my
                        face like a wall, but I can't really feel it either, you know; and then I get a terrible
                        headache," or "my nasal sinuses
                        are making themselves felt," etc. (Reich 1952a, p. 452)

Typical of the deadly orgone sickness is an "emotional dullness," a
flagging capacity to endure, which in some cases appears in the form of
dazedness. This goes hand in hand with a dull expression in the
eyes, with an expression of despair in the facial features, of which the victim
need not necessarily be aware.

From this it follows, as Reich has shown, that drought and desert are not purely
"geographical" problems—for when everything has been said about the outer desert,
there still remains for us the desert that is within, the drying-up in the heart of
man that makes such things as hydrogen bombs inevitable.

"We are facing a critical situation," Reich writes in 1954, "not only as the species
man; rather, the life principle as such is threatened on earth. We
find ourselves in the midst of a deep and decisive process of upheaval that

96
     Paul Ritter's Orgonomic Functionalism, "People and weather."

www.orgonomie.net
                                       233

concerns our entire biological, physical, emotional, and cosmic existence"
(Reich 1954b, p. 53).

Is it any wonder that the visitors from space are so interested in us,
at this particular time in our history?

www.orgonomie.net
                                        234


---


One last intriguing possibility remains. We know nothing certain about the
extraterrestrials who have landed on earth. I say this with due
respect for the reports of Adamski, Allingham, and others. We are therefore not entitled to draw any conclusions about the kind of life
that the occupants may live. Gerald Heard has proposed that the only kind
of life that will have developed on other planets would be of the type of very
intelligent bees. Other experts seem to think that a
reasonably human kind of being is entirely possible.

If they are indeed human, the first question we must put to them is what
their intentions are—whether they wish to harm us or to help us. So far, after eight
years of sightings, nothing has occurred that could be interpreted as deliberate
hostility. The question forces itself upon one involuntarily: do they
share with us many of the particular "human" qualities that we have
developed—things like the little quarrels between nations and the great brutalities
on the battlefields, our emotional sickness, our apathy in the face of
catastrophes, and the hard, narrow way in which we treat our children. In
one word, all that Reich meant when he described the armor
that keeps life from flowing freely in human beings. Will the space people also be armored like us? …and do they print caricatures
of us in their comics, with green horns and diabolically distorted features?

The great hope is that they are not armored, and that the hydrogen bomb
is as foreign to their way of life as, so far, the orgone energy is to ours. If they
know how to handle orgone in space, may we then assume that
they will also know of its other properties, which are no less important for us—its use as a gentle healing energy, its slow
growth and development as life energy in the bodies of the young?
If that is the case, they could one day teach us a great deal about how
one can combat the desolate death in the atmosphere, and how one can more effectively prevent the desolate death
in the heart. They could do this because they would understand what orgone
research has so compellingly shown—the fundamental unity that underlies the
living, emotional quality of human beings and the non-living
physical properties of matter and space. The energy that
permeates every cell and every tissue of the living body is the same
energy that drives the universe itself. The discovery of orgone has thus removed
the barrier that almost always existed between a human organism and
the cosmic environment in which it is rooted and in which it has its
origin.

www.orgonomie.net
